# DB1 Personal and Daily Life
*Master Database for IELTS Speaking*

---

# TOPIC: HOMETOWN

#### 1. PERSONA POOL

[P1] **Local Resident**: Friendly, proud of their hometown, speaks in a relaxed and descriptive way.
[P2] **Outsider/Visitor**: Curious, observant, compares the place to other cities.
[P3] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

#### 2. QUESTION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: Where is your hometown?
- Q_5_02: What do you like about your hometown?
- Q_5_03: Is your hometown a good place to live?

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `quiet and peaceful`: calm and without noise.
- `friendly people`: nice and helpful people.
- `fresh air`: clean air, not polluted.
- `relaxing atmosphere`: a calm environment.
- `delicious and affordable food`: great taste at a reasonable price.
- `strong sense of community`: a close community where people help each other.
- `limited job opportunities`: not many jobs available.

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: "My hometown is [adjective] because it's [adjective] and [adjective]."
- Pattern_2: "I like [noun] because it's [adjective]. Also, [clause]."

# TOPIC: DAILY ROUTINE

#### 1. PERSONA POOL

[P1] **Organized Person**: Values structure and time management in their daily life.
[P2] **Flexible Individual**: Prefers a loose schedule and adapts to the day's demands.
[P3] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

#### 2. QUESTION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: What do you usually do in a day?
- Q_5_02: Do you have a fixed daily routine?
- Q_5_03: What part of the day do you enjoy the most?

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `wake up early`: to get up early in the morning.
- `relax by reading books`: to find rest and peace by reading.
- `follow a regular schedule`: to have a set routine.
- `stay organized`: to be well-ordered and structured.
- `make small changes`: to change some minor aspects of a routine.
- `relax after a long day`: to unwind after a tiring day.
- `spend time with`: to use time being with someone.

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: "On weekdays, I [verb] and [verb]. In the evening, I [verb]."
- Pattern_2: "I follow a [adjective] schedule because it helps me [verb]."
- Pattern_3: "I enjoy [time] the most because I can [verb]."

# TOPIC: HOBBIES

#### 1. PERSONA POOL

[P1] **Enthusiastic Hobbyist**: Passionate about their pastimes, sees them as a source of happiness and learning.
[P2] **Casual Participant**: Engages in hobbies for relaxation, not deeply committed.
[P3] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

#### 2. QUESTION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: What hobbies do you have?
- Q_5_02: How often do you do your hobbies?
- Q_5_03: Do you think hobbies are important?

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `help me relax`: to make me feel calm.
- `learn new things`: to acquire new knowledge or skills.
- `in my free time`: when I am not busy.
- `a few times a year`: several times per year.
- `reduce stress`: to lower feelings of worry.
- `develop new skills`: to learn and build new abilities.
- `like-minded individuals`: people with similar interests.

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: "I enjoy [verb-ing], [verb-ing], and [verb-ing]."
- Pattern_2: "These activities help me [verb] and [verb] at the same time."
- Pattern_3: "Hobbies are important because they help people [verb] and [verb]."

# TOPIC: FOOD

#### 1. PERSONA POOL

[P1] **Food Enthusiast**: Loves exploring traditional and local cuisine, appreciates fresh ingredients.
[P2] **Health-Conscious Eater**: Prefers eating home-cooked meals for health and affordability.
[P3] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

#### 2. QUESTION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: What kind of food do you like?
- Q_5_02: Do you prefer eating at home or in restaurants?
- Q_5_03: What is a popular dish in your country?

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `full of flavor`: rich in taste.
- `home-cooked food`: food prepared at home.
- `try new dishes`: to taste food you've never had before.
- `popular dish`: a well-loved and commonly eaten meal.
- `delicious, flavorful broth`: a tasty and rich soup liquid.

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: "I love [type of food] because it's [adjective] and [adjective]."
- Pattern_2: "I prefer [verb-ing] at home because it's [adjective] and [adjective]."
- Pattern_3: "[Dish] is one of the most famous dishes in [country]."

# TOPIC: SHOPPING

#### 1. PERSONA POOL

[P1] **Mindful Shopper**: Enjoys shopping but is careful with money, prefers online convenience.
[P2] **In-Store Shopper**: Likes the physical experience of trying items on before buying.
[P3] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

#### 2. QUESTION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: Do you like shopping?
- Q_5_02: Do you prefer shopping online or in stores?
- Q_5_03: What's the last thing you bought?

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `explore different styles`: to try out various fashion looks.
- `find good deals`: to get items at a low price.
- `careful with my spending`: to not spend too much money.
- `compare prices`: to look at costs of different items.
- `read reviews`: to see what other people think of a product.
- `try them on first`: to put on clothes to test the fit.
- `personal development`: activities that improve skills and character.

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: "I enjoy shopping, especially for [items]. However, I try to [verb]."
- Pattern_2: "I prefer [method] because it's [adjective]."
- Pattern_3: "The last thing I bought was a [item] about [subject]."

# TOPIC: TRAVEL

#### 1. PERSONA POOL

[P1] **Adventurous Traveler**: Loves exploring new places, cultures, and food; has a bucket list.
[P2] **Social Traveler**: Prefers traveling with family and friends for safety and shared experiences.
[P3] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

#### 2. QUESTION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: Do you like traveling?
- Q_5_02: Where would you like to travel in the future?
- Q_5_03: Do you prefer traveling alone or with others?

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `explore new places`: to go to and discover new locations.
- `meet new people`: to get to know new individuals.
- `experience different cultures`: to understand the customs of various groups.
- `beautiful scenery`: attractive views of nature.
- `rich history`: a lot of past events and stories.
- `enjoy personal time`: to have time just for oneself.

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: "I love traveling because it allows me to [verb], [verb], and [verb]."
- Pattern_2: "I'd love to visit [country] because of its [nouns]."
- Pattern_3: "I prefer [verb-ing] with [people] because it's [adjective]."

# TOPIC: ACCOMMODATION

#### 1. PERSONA POOL

[P1] **Comfortable Resident**: Lives in a cozy apartment and appreciates its convenience and view.
[P2] **Future Planner**: Hopes to move to a larger house with a garden for more space.
[P3] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

#### 2. QUESTION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: Do you live in a house or an apartment?
- Q_5_02: What do you like about your home?
- Q_5_03: Would you like to move to a different home in the future?

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `small but comfortable`: not large, but pleasant.
- `located near my workplace`: close to where I work.
- `cozy and quiet`: comfortable and silent.
- `safe neighborhood`: an area free from danger.
- `nice view from the balcony`: a good scene visible from the balcony.
- `a great place to relax`: a nice area to unwind.
- `bigger house with a garden`: a larger home with an outdoor space.

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: "I live in a [type]. It's [adjective] but [adjective], and it has [clause]."
- Pattern_2: "I like that it's [adjective] and [adjective]. It's a great place to [verb]."
- Pattern_3: "In the future, I'd love to [verb] because [clause]."

# TOPIC: FRIENDS

#### 1. PERSONA POOL

[P1] **Social Butterfly**: Has a small, close-knit friend group and values quality over quantity.
[P2] **Active Socializer**: Meets friends regularly for shared activities.
[P3] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

#### 2. QUESTION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: Do you have many friends?
- Q_5_02: How often do you meet your friends?
- Q_5_03: What do you usually do with your friends?

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `small group of close friends`: a limited number of good friends.
- `quality over quantity`: focusing on the value of things rather than number.
- `real friendship is about trust and understanding`: a true friendship is based on confidence and empathy.
- `at least once a week`: a minimum of one time per week.
- `hang out and talk`: to spend time together and chat.

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: "I have a [adjective] group of friends. I prefer [noun] over [noun]."
- Pattern_2: "We usually go out for [activity] or just [verb] and [verb]."
- Pattern_3: "Spending time with friends helps me [verb] and [verb]."

# TOPIC: PETS

#### 1. PERSONA POOL

[P1] **Animal Lover**: Wants a pet for companionship and sees them as important for emotional health.
[P2] **Future Pet Owner**: Plans to get a dog for its loyalty and playfulness.
[P3] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

#### 2. QUESTION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: Do you have a pet?
- Q_5_02: What pet would you like to have?
- Q_5_03: Do you think pets are important?

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `at the moment`: currently.
- `would love to have`: wish to own.
- `loyal and playful`: faithful and fun-loving.
- `great for companionship`: good for having a friend.
- `bring happiness`: to provide joy.
- `reduce stress`: to lower tension.
- `teach people responsibility`: to help people learn to be accountable.

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: "No, I don't, but I would love to have one in the future, maybe a [pet]."
- Pattern_2: "I would like to have a [pet] because they are [adjective] and [adjective]."
- Pattern_3: "Pets are important because they [verb] and [verb]."

# TOPIC: READING

#### 1. PERSONA POOL

[P1] **Avid Reader**: Values reading for learning and using imagination; prefers paper books.
[P2] **Practical Reader**: Reads for self-improvement and convenience, especially when traveling.
[P3] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

#### 2. QUESTION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: Do you like reading books?
- Q_5_02: What kind of books do you like to read?
- Q_5_03: Do you prefer paper books or e-books?

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `learn new things`: to obtain new knowledge.
- `improves my imagination`: to enhance creativity.
- `usually read before bed`: to often read at night before sleeping.
- `novels and self-help books`: storybooks and books for personal growth.
- `entertaining and helpful`: amusing and beneficial.
- `the feeling of holding a real book`: the physical sensation of a paper book.
- `more convenient when traveling`: easier to use during trips.

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: "I love reading because it helps me [verb] and [verb]."
- Pattern_2: "I like reading [genres] because they are [adjective] and [adjective]."
- Pattern_3: "I prefer [books] because I like [clause]. However, [books] are more [adjective]."

# TOPIC: WEEKEND ACTIVITIES

#### 1. PERSONA POOL

[P1] **Relaxing Socializer**: Enjoys meeting friends and relaxing at home on weekends.
[P2] **Explorer**: Likes to go out and explore new places depending on their mood.
[P3] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

#### 2. QUESTION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: What do you usually do on weekends?
- Q_5_02: Do you prefer spending weekends indoors or outdoors?
- Q_5_03: Do you think weekends are important?

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `relax, meet friends`: to rest and socialize.
- `stay home and watch movies`: to remain at home and view films.
- `depends on my mood`: varies with how I feel.
- `explore new places`: to go to new locations.
- `stay at home to rest`: to remain home and unwind.
- `recharge after a busy week`: to recover energy after a tiring period.

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: "I usually [verb], [verb], or [verb]."
- Pattern_2: "It depends on my mood. Sometimes I like [verb-ing], but other times I prefer [verb-ing]."
- Pattern_3: "Yes, weekends help people [verb] and [verb] after a busy week."

# TOPIC: MOBILE PHONES

#### 1. PERSONA POOL

[P1] **Dependent User**: Uses their phone extensively every day for various functions.
[P2] **Mindful User**: Recognizes the overuse of phones and advocates for healthy breaks.
[P3] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

#### 2. QUESTION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: Do you use your phone often?
- Q_5_02: What do you use your phone for?
- Q_5_03: Do you think people use their phones too much?

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `every day for work, communication, and entertainment`: used daily for various activities.
- `important part of my daily life`: significant in my routine.
- `texting, watching videos, listening to music`: sending messages, seeing content, and hearing audio.
- `browsing the internet`: looking at online content.
- `stay connected with everything`: be in touch with all aspects of life.
- `take breaks and use them wisely`: to rest and use responsibly.

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: "I use my phone every day for [purposes]. It's an important part of my daily life."
- Pattern_2: "I use it for [activities]. It helps me [verb]."
- Pattern_3: "Yes, many people spend too much time on their phones, which is not good for their health."

# TOPIC: Dreams

## 1. PERSONA POOL
- [P1] **Passionate Advocate**: Extremely enthusiastic about the topic. Believes strongly in its positive impacts and readily shares detailed examples and personal experiences.
- [P2] **Objective Analyst**: Takes a balanced, analytical view. Weighs both pros and cons, often citing studies, societal trends, or broader implications rather than purely personal feelings.
- [P3] **Skeptic / Critic**: Focuses on the negative aspects, challenges, or unintended consequences associated with the topic. Often highlights what goes wrong or what needs improvement.
- [P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

## 2. QUESTION POOL
### Band 6.0 - 8.5
- Q_01: What are some of the main benefits or positive aspects of dreams in modern life?
- Q_02: Can you describe any challenges or negative consequences related to dreams?
- Q_03: How has the role of dreams changed in recent years compared to the past?
- Q_04: What impact does dreams have on our society and daily habits?
- Q_05: What do you think the future holds regarding dreams?

## 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL
### Band 6.0 - 8.5
- **wake up in a pool of sweat**: thức dậy đầm đìa mồ hôi
- **a symbol of fear**: biểu tượng của sự sợ hãi
- **let yourself go**: dừng lại nghỉ ngơi
- **enjoy life**: tận hưởng cuộc sống
- **dream interpreters**: những người giải mộng
- **be worried about**: lo lắng về điều gì
- **dreamer**: người nằm mơ
- **reflect**: phản ánh

## 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS
- Pattern_1: "Dreams about falling can be a symbol of fear, representing failure at work or love, and suggest a need to let yourself go." (Using advanced topic-specific vocabulary to elaborate on dreams)
- Pattern_2: "Dreams about being chased often mean you are trying to avoid something in daily life." (Using advanced topic-specific vocabulary to elaborate on dreams)
- Pattern_3: "Losing teeth in dreams might reflect worries about attractiveness or appearance." (Using advanced topic-specific vocabulary to elaborate on dreams)
- Pattern_4: "Dreams about dying can reflect anxiety about change or a fear of the unknown." (Using advanced topic-specific vocabulary to elaborate on dreams)

# TOPIC: Sleep

## 1. PERSONA POOL
- [P1] **Passionate Advocate**: Extremely enthusiastic about the topic. Believes strongly in its positive impacts and readily shares detailed examples and personal experiences.
- [P2] **Objective Analyst**: Takes a balanced, analytical view. Weighs both pros and cons, often citing studies, societal trends, or broader implications rather than purely personal feelings.
- [P3] **Skeptic / Critic**: Focuses on the negative aspects, challenges, or unintended consequences associated with the topic. Often highlights what goes wrong or what needs improvement.
- [P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

## 2. QUESTION POOL
### Band 6.0 - 8.5
- Q_01: What are some of the main benefits or positive aspects of sleep in modern life?
- Q_02: Can you describe any challenges or negative consequences related to sleep?
- Q_03: How has the role of sleep changed in recent years compared to the past?
- Q_04: What impact does sleep have on our society and daily habits?
- Q_05: What do you think the future holds regarding sleep?

## 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL
### Band 6.0 - 8.5
- **getting a good night's sleep**: có được một giấc ngủ ngon
- **on a regular basis**: một cách thường xuyên
- **quality sleep**: giấc ngủ chất lượng
- **raise the risk of**: làm tăng nguy cơ
- **heart disease**: bệnh tim
- **stroke**: đột quỵ
- **obesity**: béo phì
- **dementia**: chứng mất trí nhớ
- **myth**: lầm tưởng
- **recommend**: khuyến nghị

## 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS
- Pattern_1: "The pace of modern life makes getting a good night's sleep on a regular basis seem like a dream." (Using advanced topic-specific vocabulary to elaborate on sleep)
- Pattern_2: "Not getting enough quality sleep raises the risk of heart disease, stroke, obesity, and dementia." (Using advanced topic-specific vocabulary to elaborate on sleep)
- Pattern_3: "Sleep is not just 'down time'; it helps prepare your brain to learn, remember, and create." (Using advanced topic-specific vocabulary to elaborate on sleep)
- Pattern_4: "Experts recommend school-age children get at least nine hours, and adults need at least seven hours." (Using advanced topic-specific vocabulary to elaborate on sleep)

# TOPIC: Shopping

## 1. PERSONA POOL
- [P1] **Passionate Advocate**: Extremely enthusiastic about the topic. Believes strongly in its positive impacts and readily shares detailed examples and personal experiences.
- [P2] **Objective Analyst**: Takes a balanced, analytical view. Weighs both pros and cons, often citing studies, societal trends, or broader implications rather than purely personal feelings.
- [P3] **Skeptic / Critic**: Focuses on the negative aspects, challenges, or unintended consequences associated with the topic. Often highlights what goes wrong or what needs improvement.
- [P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

## 2. QUESTION POOL
### Band 6.0 - 8.5
- Q_01: What are some of the main benefits or positive aspects of shopping in modern life?
- Q_02: Can you describe any challenges or negative consequences related to shopping?
- Q_03: How has the role of shopping changed in recent years compared to the past?
- Q_04: What impact does shopping have on our society and daily habits?
- Q_05: What do you think the future holds regarding shopping?

## 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL
### Band 6.0 - 8.5
- **retail therapy**: liệu pháp mua sắm
- **boost your mood**: cải thiện tâm trạng
- **feeling low**: cảm thấy chán nản
- **crave something**: thèm thuồng
- **impulse**: sự thôi thúc
- **arise**: phát sinh
- **frustration**: thất vọng
- **loss of control**: mất kiểm soát
- **conflict**: xung đột
- **counter-productive**: phản tác dụng

## 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS
- Pattern_1: "Retail therapy is the act of buying something to boost your mood when feeling low." (Using advanced topic-specific vocabulary to elaborate on shopping)
- Pattern_2: "When stressed, people tend to crave treats and feel stronger impulses to shop." (Using advanced topic-specific vocabulary to elaborate on shopping)
- Pattern_3: "Chronically using shopping for mood enhancement can lead to unintended issues arising." (Using advanced topic-specific vocabulary to elaborate on shopping)
- Pattern_4: "Compulsive buying can cause debt, frustration, loss of control, and conflict, which is counter-productive." (Using advanced topic-specific vocabulary to elaborate on shopping)

# TOPIC: Money

## 1. PERSONA POOL
- [P1] **Passionate Advocate**: Extremely enthusiastic about the topic. Believes strongly in its positive impacts and readily shares detailed examples and personal experiences.
- [P2] **Objective Analyst**: Takes a balanced, analytical view. Weighs both pros and cons, often citing studies, societal trends, or broader implications rather than purely personal feelings.
- [P3] **Skeptic / Critic**: Focuses on the negative aspects, challenges, or unintended consequences associated with the topic. Often highlights what goes wrong or what needs improvement.
- [P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

## 2. QUESTION POOL
### Band 6.0 - 8.5
- Q_01: What are some of the main benefits or positive aspects of money in modern life?
- Q_02: Can you describe any challenges or negative consequences related to money?
- Q_03: How has the role of money changed in recent years compared to the past?
- Q_04: What impact does money have on our society and daily habits?
- Q_05: What do you think the future holds regarding money?

## 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL
### Band 6.0 - 8.5
- **more bang for your buck(s)**: sự đầu tư mang lại nhiều lợi nhuận
- **budget**: ngân sách
- **track**: theo dõi
- **in advance**: trước
- **balloon**: làm tăng
- **expenditure**: chi tiêu
- **impulse purchase**: mua sắm bốc đồng
- **sleep deprivation**: tình trạng thiếu ngủ
- **stick to**: tuân thủ
- **bargain bin**: thùng hàng giá hời

## 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS
- Pattern_1: "Spending money wisely enables you to get more bang for your buck." (Using advanced topic-specific vocabulary to elaborate on money)
- Pattern_2: "Create a budget and track your spending to get an accurate picture of your finances." (Using advanced topic-specific vocabulary to elaborate on money)
- Pattern_3: "Plan purchases in advance, as spur of the moment decisions can balloon your expenditures." (Using advanced topic-specific vocabulary to elaborate on money)
- Pattern_4: "Avoid impulse purchases when judgement is impaired by sleep deprivation, and wait for items to hit the bargain bin." (Using advanced topic-specific vocabulary to elaborate on money)

# TOPIC: Family

## 1. PERSONA POOL
- [P1] **Passionate Advocate**: Extremely enthusiastic about the topic. Believes strongly in its positive impacts and readily shares detailed examples and personal experiences.
- [P2] **Objective Analyst**: Takes a balanced, analytical view. Weighs both pros and cons, often citing studies, societal trends, or broader implications rather than purely personal feelings.
- [P3] **Skeptic / Critic**: Focuses on the negative aspects, challenges, or unintended consequences associated with the topic. Often highlights what goes wrong or what needs improvement.
- [P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

## 2. QUESTION POOL
### Band 6.0 - 8.5
- Q_01: What are some of the main benefits or positive aspects of family in modern life?
- Q_02: Can you describe any challenges or negative consequences related to family?
- Q_03: How has the role of family changed in recent years compared to the past?
- Q_04: What impact does family have on our society and daily habits?
- Q_05: What do you think the future holds regarding family?

## 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL
### Band 6.0 - 8.5
- **bond**: mối quan hệ
- **set aside time for**: dành thời gian cho
- **designate**: dành cho, chỉ định
- **electronics**: thiết bị điện tử
- **chore**: việc vặt

## 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_3: "Eat meals together without electronics to encourage meaningful conversations." (Using advanced topic-specific vocabulary to elaborate on family)
- Pattern_4: "Do chores as a family by creating a list and having everyone tackle their chores at the same time." (Using advanced topic-specific vocabulary to elaborate on family)

# TOPIC: Holidays

## 1. PERSONA POOL
- [P1] **Passionate Advocate**: Extremely enthusiastic about the topic. Believes strongly in its positive impacts and readily shares detailed examples and personal experiences.
- [P2] **Objective Analyst**: Takes a balanced, analytical view. Weighs both pros and cons, often citing studies, societal trends, or broader implications rather than purely personal feelings.
- [P3] **Skeptic / Critic**: Focuses on the negative aspects, challenges, or unintended consequences associated with the topic. Often highlights what goes wrong or what needs improvement.
- [P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

## 2. QUESTION POOL
### Band 6.0 - 8.5
- Q_01: What are some of the main benefits or positive aspects of holidays in modern life?
- Q_02: Can you describe any challenges or negative consequences related to holidays?
- Q_03: How has the role of holidays changed in recent years compared to the past?
- Q_04: What impact does holidays have on our society and daily habits?
- Q_05: What do you think the future holds regarding holidays?

## 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL
### Band 6.0 - 8.5
- **occupy oneself**: làm cho bản thân bận rộn
- **waste your time**: lãng phí thời gian
- **broaden/expand/widen someone's horizons**: mở rộng tầm nhìn
- **companion**: người bạn đồng hành
- **self-improvement**: cải thiện bản thân
- **devote**: dành thời gian
- **loved one**: người thân yêu
- **have a quality talk with**: có cuộc nói chuyện ý nghĩa

## 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS
- Pattern_1: "Don't waste your time during holidays; find things to occupy yourself and broaden your horizons." (Using advanced topic-specific vocabulary to elaborate on holidays)
- Pattern_2: "Go on an adventure with a companion to explore new places." (Using advanced topic-specific vocabulary to elaborate on holidays)
- Pattern_3: "Devote time to self-improvement by learning new skills like dancing or playing the piano." (Using advanced topic-specific vocabulary to elaborate on holidays)
- Pattern_4: "Holidays are a great opportunity to visit loved ones and have a quality talk with them." (Using advanced topic-specific vocabulary to elaborate on holidays)

# TOPIC: Change and consequences

## 1. PERSONA POOL
- [P1] **Nostalgic Resident**: Has lived in the same place for a long time. Remembers when things were quieter and less crowded. Focuses on the negative consequences of change like traffic and noise.
- [P2] **Urban Enthusiast**: Enjoys the dynamic, vibrant nature of city life. Appreciates new facilities, modern architecture, and the convenience of living centrally.
- [P3] **Pragmatic Student**: Currently navigating changes like moving away from home, starting university, and finding accommodation. Focuses on practical aspects like transport and costs.
- [P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

## 2. QUESTION POOL
### Band 4.5 - 6.0
- Q_5_01: Could you tell me something about where you live?
- Q_5_02: What sort of place is that?
- Q_5_03: Is there anything you particularly like about it?
- Q_5_04: And what kind of jobs do people do there?
- Q_5_05: What form of transport do you use most?
- Q_5_06: Has the type of building changed since you were a child? How?

## 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL
### Band 4.5 - 6.0
- **shared house**: a house that people live in and share the rent and bills.
- **studio**: a small apartment containing one main room.
- **farmhouse**: the main house on a farm, usually where the farmer lives.
- **house**: a building for human habitation, especially one that is lived in by a family or small group of people.
- **flat**: a set of rooms forming an individual residence, typically on one floor and within a larger building containing a number of such residences.
- **bungalow**: a low house, with a broad front porch, having either no upper floor or upper rooms set in the roof, typically with dormer windows.
- **penthouse**: an apartment on the top floor of a tall building, typically luxuriously fitted and offering fine views.
- **terraced house**: a house built as part of a continuous row in a uniform style.
- **fascinating**: extremely interesting.
- **cramped**: feeling or causing someone to feel uncomfortably confined or hemmed in by lack of space.
- **modern**: relating to the present or recent times as opposed to the remote past.
- **bustling**: (of a place) full of activity.
- **uncomfortable**: causing or feeling slight pain or physical discomfort.
- **shabby**: in poor condition through long or hard use or lack of care.
- **boring**: not interesting; tedious.

## 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS
- Pattern_1: "I used to live in Switzerland but I've recently moved to the UK." (Using 'used to' for past states and present perfect for recent changes)
- Pattern_2: "It's a large, busy place. It's near London." (Using simple adjectives to describe a place)
- Pattern_3: "What I like about it is the facilities... because you've got everything you need." (Using 'What I like about it is... because...' to explain a preference)
- Pattern_4: "The great thing about Weybridge is the facilities." (Using 'The great thing about... is...' to emphasize a positive point)
- Pattern_5: "I suppose most people work in offices, for big companies." (Using 'I suppose' to indicate a generalization or guess)

topic_id: "the-importance-of-the-past"
topic_name: "The importance of the past"
target_levels: ["4.5-6.0"]
---

---

# TOPIC: The importance of the past

## 1. PERSONA POOL
- [P1] **Sentimental Storyteller**: Cherishes personal memories and objects with sentimental value (heirlooms, photographs). Enjoys recounting past experiences and adventures in detail.
- [P2] **Practical Learner**: Views the past, both personal and historical, as a source of lessons for the future. Focuses on what can be learned from past mistakes or achievements.
- [P3] **History Buff**: Fascinated by different types of history (social, military, etc.) and ancient times. Believes understanding history is crucial to understanding the modern world.
- [P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

## 2. QUESTION POOL
### Band 4.5 - 6.0
- Q_5_01: Tell me about an achievement.
- Q_5_02: What kind of event was it?
- Q_5_03: What happened during the experience?
- Q_5_04: Describe an important possession that you lost.
- Q_5_05: Describe a time when you were late for an event.

## 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL
### Band 4.5 - 6.0
- **unforgettable journey**: a trip that is impossible to forget; very memorable.
- **happy memories**: thoughts of past experiences that bring joy.
- **sentimental value**: importance to someone because of a connection with a happy time of life, a special person, etc.
- **rewarding experience**: an experience that provides satisfaction.
- **bizarre incident**: a very strange or unusual event.
- **memorable trip**: a journey that is worth remembering.
- **formal occasion**: a special event that requires formal dress and behavior.
- **nerve-racking adventure**: an exciting experience that causes stress or anxiety.
- **exhilarating experience**: a thrilling and exciting experience.
- **humbling experience**: an experience that makes you realize you are not as important or special as you thought.
- **traditional**: existing in or as part of a tradition; long-established.
- **modern**: relating to the present or recent times.
- **look to the future**: to focus on what will happen later, rather than what is happening now or what has happened in the past.
- **old-fashioned**: in or according to styles or types no longer current or common; not modern.

## 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS
- Pattern_1: "I remember one time when I lost a handbag." (Introducing a narrative about the past)
- Pattern_2: "Well, I should say first that it was probably my own fault." (Providing context or evaluation of a past event)
- Pattern_3: "Looking back, I realize that I had a habit of putting it down." (Reflecting on past habits using 'Looking back, I realize that')
- Pattern_4: "Anyway, to find it, I first went back to the shop, but they couldn't help. After that, I went to the police... so finally, that's what I did." (Using sequencing words: 'first', 'After that', 'finally' to tell a story)
- Pattern_5: "In future, I won't use a handbag. I'll always use a bag with a shoulder strap." (Drawing a lesson for the future using 'In future, I will/won't')
- Pattern_6: "In future, I will keep a paper map in the car in case my GPS gives me wrong information." (Describing precautions using 'in case')

topic_name: "Machines, cycles and processes"
target_levels: ["4.5-6.0"]

# TOPIC: Machines, cycles and processes

## 1. PERSONA POOL
- [P1] **Consumer/Shopper**: Frequently buys things online or in stores. Has experiences with faulty products, returning items, and interacting with customer service or repair processes.
- [P2] **Eco-Advocate**: Highly aware of product life cycles, manufacturing processes, and the environmental impact of packaging and waste. Advocates for recycling and sustainable practices.
- [P3] **Trend Follower**: Observes the cycles of fashion and consumerism. Notices how people buy things not just out of necessity, but to "keep up with the Joneses."
- [P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

## 2. QUESTION POOL
### Band 4.5 - 6.0
- Q_5_01: Is it safe to shop over the Internet?
- Q_5_02: What do you think the main effects of Internet shopping are?
- Q_5_03: Have you bought anything over the Internet? If not, would you?
- Q_5_04: Have you ever bought anything which was damaged? What did you do?
- Q_5_05: In what ways does fashion affect different aspects of our lives?
- Q_5_06: What makes people follow fashion?
- Q_5_07: To what extent does how we dress indicate who we are?

## 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL
### Band 4.5 - 6.0
- **damaged**: harmed or spoiled.
- **broken**: having been fractured or damaged and no longer in one piece or in working order.
- **torn**: (of cloth or paper) pulled apart or to pieces.
- **ripped**: badly torn.
- **scratched**: having a mark or marks on the surface caused by scraping with a sharp or pointed object.
- **cracked**: damaged and showing lines on the surface from having split without coming apart.
- **jammed**: stuck and unable to move.
- **leaking**: (of a container or covering) accidentally losing or admitting contents, especially liquid or gas, through a hole or crack.
- **cotton**: a soft white fibrous substance that surrounds the seeds of a tropical and subtropical plant and is used as textile fiber and thread for sewing.
- **gold**: a yellow precious metal.
- **plastic**: a synthetic material made from a wide range of organic polymers.
- **spherical**: shaped like a sphere.
- **rectangular**: denoting or shaped like a rectangle.
- **circular**: having the form of a circle.
- **advertising**: the activity or profession of producing advertisements for commercial products or services.
- **convenience**: the state of being able to proceed with something with little effort or difficulty.

## 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS
- Pattern_1: "You sent me these trousers, and I was really annoyed to find that the trouser leg was completely ripped." (Complaining about a product using 'annoyed to find that')
- Pattern_2: "When I opened the food blender you sent, I found the bowl was leaking very badly." (Describing a problem found upon opening)
- Pattern_3: "In my view, there is too much advertising on television." (Using 'In my view' to state an opinion)
- Pattern_4: "To my mind, advertising is fun." (Using 'To my mind' to state an opinion)
- Pattern_5: "It seems to me that advertising does more harm than good." (Using 'It seems to me that' to express a perspective)
- Pattern_6: "My impression is that most advertising is misleading." (Using 'My impression is that' to state a belief based on observation)
- Pattern_7: "I suppose that the main difference is that it's set up to be a lot more convenient." (Using 'I suppose that' to offer a reasoned guess or opinion)

# TOPIC: Life choices

## 1. PERSONA POOL
- [P1] **Recent Graduate**: Young, enthusiastic, currently taking a gap year or looking for a first job. Talks about degrees, independence, and getting a place of their own.
- [P2] **Mid-Career Professional**: Established in their job but looking for a change. Might talk about taking a sabbatical, changing careers, or balancing work with family life.
- [P3] **Retiree/Older Adult**: Looking back on a fulfilling life. Mentions early retirement, momentous life decisions, and doing voluntary work in their spare time.
- [P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

## 2. QUESTION POOL
### Band 5.0 - 6.0
- Q_5_01: How did you get here today?
- Q_5_02: What do you like about your home town?
- Q_5_03: Do you live in a house or flat?
- Q_5_04: Do you prefer eating at home or in a restaurant?
- Q_5_05: How long have you been learning English?
- Q_5_06: Do you play any sports?
- Q_5_07: Describe an experience or event in your life.

### Band 6.5+
- Q_7_01: What was a challenging experience you remember?
- Q_7_02: What was a life-changing decision you made?
- Q_7_03: What was a frustrating experience you had?

## 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL
### Band 5.0 - 6.0
- **degree**: a course of study at a university or college, or the qualification that is given to you when you have successfully completed the course.
- **driving test**: the official test that you must pass in order to be legally allowed to drive on public roads.
- **family**: a group consisting of one or two parents and their children.
- **a place of your own**: a home that you own or rent yourself, rather than living with your parents.
- **start a family**: to have your first child.
- **disappointing**: not as good as you had hoped or expected; causing disappointment.
- **memorable**: worth remembering or easily remembered, especially because of being special or unusual.
- **challenging**: testing one's abilities; demanding.
- **tough**: physically or emotionally difficult.
- **straightforward**: uncomplicated and easy to do or understand.
- **ordinary**: with no special or distinctive features; normal.
- **dull**: lacking interest or excitement.
- **exciting**: causing great enthusiasm and eagerness.

### Band 6.5+
- **career**: an occupation undertaken for a significant period of a person's life and with opportunities for progress.
- **voluntary work**: work done without payment, usually for a charity or community organization.
- **gap year**: a period, typically an academic year, taken by a student as a break between school and university or higher education.
- **sabbatical**: a period of paid leave granted to a university teacher or other worker for study or travel, traditionally one year for every seven years worked.
- **early retirement**: the practice of leaving employment before the statutory age, especially on a favorable financial basis.
- **rewarding**: providing satisfaction; gratifying.
- **fulfilling**: making someone satisfied or happy because of fully developing their character or abilities.
- **unremarkable**: not particularly interesting or surprising.
- **unforgettable**: impossible to forget; very memorable.
- **once in a lifetime**: very special because it will probably only happen once.
- **trying**: hard to endure; testing one's patience.
- **frustrating**: causing annoyance or upset because of an inability to change or achieve something.
- **satisfying**: giving fulfillment or the pleasure associated with this.
- **stimulating**: encouraging or arousing interest or enthusiasm.
- **life-changing**: having a huge effect on someone's life.
- **insignificant**: too small or unimportant to be worth consideration.
- **momentous**: of great importance or significance, especially in having a bearing on future events.

## 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS
- Pattern_1: "I came by bus. In fact I had to take two different buses because my home is on the other side of the city..." (Using 'In fact' and 'because' to extend an answer)
- Pattern_2: "What I like most is the fact that it's near the coast because I love the sea." (Using a pseudo-cleft sentence 'What I like most is...' for emphasis)
- Pattern_3: "It depends on the situation. I love going out for a meal... Still, I think overall I prefer eating at home as my mother is an excellent cook..." (Using 'It depends' and 'Still' to show contrast)
- Pattern_4: "Well, I started learning it at primary school... so I suppose you could say I've been learning it for most of my life. But I have only been studying seriously for the last two years..." (Providing nuance with 'so I suppose' and 'But')
- Pattern_5: "Not really. I used to play basketball... but I'm not very interested in playing sports. I occasionally go to the gym... but I don't like team sports as I'm not very good at them." (Extending a negative answer with reasons)

# TOPIC: Modern communication

## 1. PERSONA POOL
- [P1] **Tech-Savvy Communicator**: Prefers text messaging, instant messaging, and social media. Thinks it's fast, convenient, and the best way to keep in touch with a large network.
- [P2] **Traditionalist**: Worries that text-speak ruins literacy. Prefers face-to-face meetings or phone calls. Believes non-verbal communication is lost in digital messages.
- [P3] **Linguist/Cultural Observer**: Fascinated by the evolution of language, the survival of minority languages, and the impact of a global lingua franca like English.
- [P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

## 2. QUESTION POOL
### Band 5.0 - 6.0
- Q_5_01: How long have you been learning English?
- Q_5_02: What for you is the most difficult thing about learning English?
- Q_5_03: Have you learnt any other foreign languages apart from English?
- Q_5_04: How important is it to learn a foreign language?
- Q_5_05: What is the most effective way to learn a language?
- Q_5_06: Describe an important letter you received.
- Q_5_07: Talk about a person you regularly communicate with but don't often see.

### Band 6.5+
- Q_7_01: Do you agree that minority languages will die out unless people make an effort to protect them?
- Q_7_02: If English is your first language, do you have greater opportunities in life?
- Q_7_03: Is cross-cultural communication difficult mainly due to different behaviour and values rather than the language barrier?
- Q_7_04: Is face-to-face communication using technology the same as talking to someone in the flesh?
- Q_7_05: Is there any point in learning a dead language like Latin?
- Q_7_06: Is the decline in the standards of written communication due to an increase in text messaging?
- Q_7_07: Are non-verbal communication skills more important than speech in conveying emotions?

## 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL
### Band 5.0 - 6.0
- **first language**: mother tongue or native language.
- **second language**: a language learned after one's native language.
- **face-to-face**: close together and facing each other.
- **written communication**: communication by means of written symbols.
- **text messaging**: the sending of short text messages electronically especially from one cell phone to another.
- **social networking**: the use of dedicated websites and applications to interact with other users.
- **convenient**: fitting in well with a person's needs, activities, and plans.
- **immediate**: occurring or done at once; instant.
- **informal**: having a relaxed, friendly, or unofficial style, manner, or nature.
- **time-consuming**: taking a lot of or too much time.

### Band 6.5+
- **minority language**: a language spoken by a minority of the population of a territory.
- **dead language**: a language which is no longer the native language of any community.
- **cross-cultural communication**: communication between people who have differences in any one of the following: styles of working, age, nationality, ethnicity, race, gender, sexual orientation, etc.
- **non-verbal communication**: the transmission of messages or signals through a nonverbal platform such as eye contact, facial expressions, gestures, posture, and the distance between two individuals.
- **official language**: a language that is given a special legal status in a particular country, state, or other jurisdiction.
- **in the flesh**: in person.
- **literacy skills**: the ability to read, write, speak and listen in a way that lets us communicate effectively and make sense of the world.
- **conveying emotions**: expressing or communicating feelings.
- **interactive**: (of two people or things) influencing or having an effect on each other.
- **impersonal**: not influenced by, showing, or involving personal feelings.

## 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS
- Pattern_1: "I'm not sure I agree with that. There are plenty of examples in history which show that this is not true..." (Using cautious disagreement and providing a counter-example)
- Pattern_2: "I agree up to a point. Even if you speak the same language, cultural differences can cause significant misunderstandings..." (Using cautious agreement with a caveat)
- Pattern_3: "I'm sure that's not right. But it all depends on how well you are taught." (Using strong disagreement followed by a condition)
- Pattern_4: "I would tend to agree. I think a knowledge of English can be very useful... but it doesn't necessarily have to be your mother tongue." (Using cautious agreement and providing a nuanced view)
- Pattern_5: "I couldn't agree more. You can tell far more about how someone is feeling from how he communicates than from what he actually says." (Expressing strong agreement and elaborating)
- Pattern_6: "There's no doubt about it. Spelling and punctuation in particular have really deteriorated..." (Expressing strong agreement with a factual observation)
- Pattern_7: "I'd like to talk about a letter I received last year." (Standard opening for a Part 2 narrative)
- Pattern_8: "What I remember most is being so excited as it was my first job offer..." (Using a pseudo-cleft sentence to emphasize a specific memory or feeling)

topic_name: "Diet and health"
target_levels: ["5.0-6.0", "6.5-7.5"]

# TOPIC: Family and friends bonds

## 1. PERSONA POOL
- [P1] **The Family Anchor**: Grew up in a large, extended family. Values strong family ties, looking back on fond memories, and believes parents are the ultimate role models.
- [P2] **The Independent Spirit**: Grew up in a smaller nuclear or single-parent family. Values deep, long-lasting friendships ("friends who go back years") as much as, or more than, family ties.
- [P3] **The Sociologist**: Analyzes trends like falling birth rates, the cost of childcare, and the changing definition of a "typical" family in modern society.
- [P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

## 2. QUESTION POOL
### Band 5.0 - 6.0
- Q_5_01: Who do you live with?
- Q_5_02: Do you have a big or small family?
- Q_5_03: Describe a person (family member, friend, or someone else you know well).
- Q_5_04: Do you help around the house as much as you could?
- Q_5_05: Do parents read to their children as much as they should?

### Band 6.5+
- Q_7_01: Do you think it's important for friends to have similar personalities?
- Q_7_02: Should couples who have children be given more financial benefits than couples who don't?
- Q_7_03: Do you think that couples who share the housework equally are likely to have fewer arguments?
- Q_7_04: Do young children who go to nursery have more opportunities for social interaction?
- Q_7_05: Do you think men tend to help more around the home than they used to?
- Q_7_06: How do you think the family unit has changed over the years?

## 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL
### Band 5.0 - 6.0
- **married couple**: a husband and wife.
- **role models**: a person looked to by others as an example to be imitated.
- **strict**: demanding that rules concerning behavior are obeyed and observed.
- **lenient**: (of a punishment or person in authority) more merciful or tolerant than expected.
- **jealousy**: the state or feeling of being jealous.
- **take after**: resemble a parent or ancestor.
- **go back (years)**: have known each other for a long time.
- **get on (well with)**: have a harmonious or friendly relationship.
- **settle down**: adopt a more steady or secure style of life, especially in a permanent job and home.
- **cheerful**: noticeably happy and optimistic.
- **ambitious**: having or showing a strong desire and determination to succeed.
- **friendly**: kind and pleasant.

### Band 6.5+
- **over-protective**: having a tendency to protect someone, especially a child, excessively.
- **sibling rivalry**: competition between siblings, often for their parents' attention.
- **childcare**: the action or skill of looking after children.
- **foster parents**: people who act as parents and guardians for a child in place of the child's natural parents but without legally adopting them.
- **widowed**: a person whose spouse has died and who has not married again.
- **single-parent families**: families with children that are headed by a single parent.
- **nuclear family**: a couple and their dependent children, regarded as a basic social unit.
- **extended family**: a family that extends beyond the nuclear family, including grandparents, aunts, uncles, and other relatives.
- **pass away**: die.
- **get over**: recover from an illness or a shock.
- **look up to**: have a great deal of respect for someone.
- **grow apart**: become less close.
- **put up with**: tolerate or endure something.
- **resourceful**: having the ability to find quick and clever ways to overcome difficulties.
- **cynical**: believing that people are motivated by self-interest; distrustful of human sincerity or integrity.

## 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS
- Pattern_1: "No I don't think so. It would be very boring and predictable if friends all had the same character." (Using substitution 'so' and 'character' for 'personality' to avoid repetition)
- Pattern_2: "I think sometimes the most interesting relationships are the ones between very different individuals." (Using substitution 'the ones' and 'individuals')
- Pattern_3: "People sometimes assume that this type of friendship can lead to arguments but they don't have to." (Using ellipsis 'don't have to [lead to arguments]')
- Pattern_4: "For example, one person could be quite sociable whilst her friend isn't..." (Using ellipsis 'isn't [sociable]')
- Pattern_5: "One of the main duties of parents is to provide their children with a loving and secure environment. Another one is to bring them up with a good sense of what is right and wrong..." (Using reference 'their' and substitution 'one' and 'them')
- Pattern_6: "The number of older people in society has increased significantly in recent years. This rise has led to a need for better healthcare provision for the elderly." (Using reference 'This' and lexical cohesion 'rise' for 'increased')
- Pattern_7: "My grandparents had a great influence on me as I was growing up, and continue to do so now, even though they are both in their eighties." (Using substitution 'do so' and reference 'they')
- Pattern_8: "Some teenagers in my school are not very well-behaved but fortunately all my friends are." (Using strong forms of verbs for emphasis: 'are')

topic_id: "money-and-shopping"
topic_name: "Money and shopping"
target_levels: ["5.0-6.0", "6.5-7.5"]
---

# TOPIC: Money and shopping

## 1. PERSONA POOL
- [P1] **Bargain Hunter/Thrifty**: Loves finding deals in second-hand shops or online auctions. Prefers to save up rather than use credit. Thinks carefully about expenditure.
- [P2] **Brand Aficionado**: Enjoys shopping in boutiques and national chain stores. Willing to pay for designer or custom-made goods. Might occasionally overspend or rely on credit cards.
- [P3] **Financial Realist**: Worried about inflation, the cost of living, and managing debt (like student loans or mortgages). Focuses on budgeting, earning a salary, and cutting back on luxuries.
- [P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

## 2. QUESTION POOL
### Band 5.0 - 6.0
- Q_5_01: What is your biggest expense/cost each month?
- Q_5_02: Do you regularly pay into a savings account?
- Q_5_03: Have you ever won or inherited money?
- Q_5_04: How often do you use national chain stores or supermarkets?
- Q_5_05: Describe something you saved up for.

### Band 6.5+
- Q_7_01: Do credit cards encourage people to buy things that they can't really afford?
- Q_7_02: Should children have to earn their pocket money/allowance?
- Q_7_03: What's the best way to cut back/economize on household spending?
- Q_7_04: Which is more important for governments to focus on - health or education expenditure?
- Q_7_05: Are there any other ways to buy things other than saving money?
- Q_7_06: Do you prefer to spend or save money?

## 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL
### Band 5.0 - 6.0
- **expense/cost**: the cost required for something; the money spent on something.
- **owe**: have an obligation to pay or repay (something, especially money) in return for something received.
- **save**: keep and store up (something, especially money) for future use.
- **pocket money/allowance**: a small amount of money given to a child by their parents, typically on a regular basis.
- **salary**: a fixed regular payment, typically paid on a monthly basis.
- **savings**: the money one has saved, especially through a bank or official scheme.
- **national chain stores**: retail outlets that share a brand and central management, and usually have standardized business methods and practices.
- **second-hand shops**: shops that sell goods that have already been owned and used.
- **refund**: a repayment of a sum of money.
- **inexpensive**: not costing a great deal; cheap.

### Band 6.5+
- **deposit account**: a bank account that pays interest and is usually not able to be drawn on without notice or loss of interest.
- **be in debit/debt**: the state of owing money.
- **expenditure**: the action of spending funds.
- **economize**: spend less; reduce one's expenses.
- **afford**: have enough money to pay for.
- **invest**: expend money with the expectation of achieving a profit or material result.
- **outgoings**: a person's regular expenditure.
- **worthless**: having no real value or use.
- **priceless**: so precious that its value cannot be determined.
- **hard up**: short of money.
- **well off**: wealthy.
- **cost-effective**: effective or productive in relation to its cost.
- **overpriced**: too expensive.
- **thrifty**: using money and other resources carefully and not wastefully.
- **extravagant**: lacking restraint in spending money or using resources.
- **made-to-measure/custom-made**: made specially to fit a particular person or space.
- **mass-produced**: produced in large quantities by an automated mechanical process.
- **off-the-peg**: (of clothes) made to a standard size and sold from stock, rather than made to measure.

## 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS
- Pattern_1: "It's certainly not impossible." (Expressing agreement using a double negative for emphasis)
- Pattern_2: "I couldn't agree with you more." (Expressing strong agreement)
- Pattern_3: "You have a point there." (Expressing cautious agreement)
- Pattern_4: "Of course they can't!" (Expressing strong disagreement)
- Pattern_5: "Surely that's a slight exaggeration?" (Expressing disagreement by questioning the premise)
- Pattern_6: "I'm not sure that's a fair assessment." (Expressing polite or cautious disagreement)
- Pattern_7: "It's never been more relevant." (Disagreeing with a statement that something is irrelevant)
- Pattern_8: "I absolutely love shopping, particularly for clothes and music so that's where most of my money goes, I'm afraid." (Answering a follow-up question with personal detail and a conversational closer 'I'm afraid')
- Pattern_9: "If you haven't got the money you might buy things on credit or take out a loan for an expensive item." (Providing alternative scenarios to answer a follow-up question)
- Pattern_10: "I suppose I was motivated to do it quickly so that helped but it still took me a long time to get enough together to afford it." (Reflecting on an experience in a follow-up answer)

# TOPIC: Family matters

## 1. PERSONA POOL
- [P1] **Nostalgic Grandparent**: A warm, reflective older person who cherishes family history, traditions, and reminisces about the "good old days." They speak fondly of a time when communities were closer.
- [P2] **Independent Young Professional**: A career-driven individual who values their family's support but prioritizes independence, personal growth, and maintaining a wide circle of friends alongside familial ties.
- [P3] **Family-Oriented Parent**: Someone deeply invested in their family unit, finding joy in family gatherings, passing down traditions, and emphasizing the importance of strong bonds and reliable support systems.
- [P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

## 2. QUESTION POOL
### Band 5.0 - 6.0
- Q_5_01: Tell me something about your family.
- Q_5_02: What do you like doing most with your family?
- Q_5_03: Who are you close to in your family?
- Q_5_04: In what way is your family important to you?
- Q_5_05: How often do you see your friend?

### Band 6.5+
- Q_7_01: In what ways have families changed in the last hundred years?
- Q_7_02: Should we rely heavily on our families or is it better to try to be independent?
- Q_7_03: The elderly are sometimes considered the wisest members of society. What do you think we can learn from them?
- Q_7_04: What do you expect from a good friend?
- Q_7_05: Are friends as important to you as family?
- Q_7_06: Do you think friendships change as we get older? How?

## 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL
### Band 5.0 - 6.0
- **ambitious**: Someone who has a strong desire to be successful, rich, or powerful.
- **blunt**: Saying exactly what you think without trying to be polite.
- **clever**: Intelligent and able to understand things easily or plan things well.
- **good fun**: Enjoyable to be with because they say and do interesting or amusing things.
- **hard-working**: Working very hard.
- **impatient**: Easily irritated by things.
- **nosy**: Interested in things which do not concern them.
- **outgoing**: Very friendly and likes meeting and talking to people.
- **reliable**: Can be trusted to work well or to behave in the way that you want them to.
- **sociable**: Friendly and enjoy talking to other people.
- **stingy**: Unwilling to spend money.
- **get on with somebody**: have a good relationship
- **be in touch with somebody**: be in communication with somebody
- **fall out with somebody**: argue and stop being friendly with somebody
- **take after somebody**: resemble somebody in your family (in appearance or personality)
- **grow up together/with somebody**: have many childhood and adolescent experiences in common with somebody
- **be close to somebody**: know somebody well and see or talk to them often

### Band 6.5+
- **creative**: Has the ability to invent and develop original ideas, especially in the arts.
- **judgemental**: Critical of them because they form opinions of people and situations very quickly.
- **open-minded**: Willing to listen to and consider other people's ideas and suggestions.
- **over-sensitive**: Too easily worried and offended when people talk about it.
- **self-assured**: Shows confidence in what they say and do because they are sure of their own abilities.
- **look up to somebody**: respect somebody
- **grow apart from somebody**: gradually have a less close relationship with somebody
- **extended family**: a family that extends beyond the nuclear family, including grandparents, aunts, uncles, and other relatives.
- **widely dispersed**: spread over a large area.
- **breakdown of communities**: the failure or collapse of community structures and relationships.
- **invaluable**: extremely useful; indispensable.
- **call on**: ask someone to do something.
- **at a moment's notice**: almost immediately.
- **living link**: a connection to the past through living people.
- **keepers of local traditions**: people who preserve and maintain cultural practices.
- **sense of identity**: the characteristics determining who or what a person or thing is.
- **life experience**: knowledge or skill gained through being involved in or exposed to life's events.
- **stood up**: failed to meet someone for a date or appointment.
- **siblings**: brothers and sisters.
- **have in common**: share interests, experiences, or other characteristics.

## 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS
- Pattern_1: "I would say I come from quite a small family, really. For example, I just have one older sister and four cousins, but it's true that my extended family is fairly big." (Using 'I would say' and 'but it's true that' for balanced descriptions)
- Pattern_2: "My family is everything to me. They are my best friends, they support me when things aren't going well and when things are going well. I know they'll always be there for me, no matter what." (Expressing strong sentiment with 'no matter what')
- Pattern_3: "In Western countries, I would say families have become more widely dispersed and less close." (Using 'I would say' to soften a generalisation)
- Pattern_4: "I think this is a shame because it means there is a breakdown of communities." (Expressing an opinion and giving a reason)
- Pattern_5: "It often seems to me that older people had harder lives and lived through more interesting periods in history, so I always enjoy asking my grandparents about their childhoods..." (Using 'It often seems to me that' for reflection)
- Pattern_6: "For me, a good friend has to be reliable. I can't stand being stood up or having my emails and texts ignored." (Expressing strong preference/dislike with 'I can't stand')
- Pattern_7: "No, I wouldn't say so. Friends come and go, but family are always there, whether you like it or not." (Disagreeing politely and using an idiom 'whether you like it or not')
- Pattern_8: "Well, I definitely find that I have less in common with some friends than I used to." (Expressing a change over time)

# TOPIC: Language and communication

## 1. PERSONA POOL
- [P1] **Language Enthusiast**: Someone who loves learning new languages, understands the nuances of different dialects, and believes language learning is essential for global understanding.
- [P2] **Traditional Communicator**: A person who prefers face-to-face meetings or phone calls over digital communication like texting or social media. They feel modern technology isolates people.
- [P3] **Global Citizen**: Values the spread of a global language like English for economic and practical reasons but also recognizes the importance of preserving local languages and cultures.
- [P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

## 2. QUESTION POOL
### Band 5.0 - 6.0
- Q_5_01: What's your mother tongue?
- Q_5_02: What other languages do you speak?
- Q_5_03: What do you think is the best way to keep in touch with friends?
- Q_5_04: Do people keep in touch differently now compared to fifty years ago?
- Q_5_05: Describe a language other than English that you would like to learn.

### Band 6.5+
- Q_7_01: Is it considered important in your country to learn foreign languages?
- Q_7_02: What, in your opinion, is the best way to learn a language?
- Q_7_03: Why are some people seemingly better at learning languages than others?
- Q_7_04: How do people in your country feel about English being the world language?
- Q_7_05: Do you think the culture of English-speaking countries, as well as the English language, dominate the world?
- Q_7_06: Why do you think people feel it is important to continue speaking their local languages?

## 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL
### Band 5.0 - 6.0
- **mother tongue**: the language that you learn from your parents when you are a baby.
- **bilingual**: able to speak two languages very well.
- **fluent**: able to speak the language easily and correctly.
- **get by**: speak a language well enough to communicate about basic things.
- **rusty**: not as good at something as you used to be because you have not done it for a long time.
- **pick up**: learn something without effort over a period of time.
- **global language**: a language that is spoken internationally.
- **minority language**: a language that is spoken by only a small proportion of people in a country.
- **foreign language**: a language that is not spoken as a native language in your country.
- **keep in touch**: write to, phone, or visit someone regularly.
- **lose touch**: gradually stop writing to, telephoning, or visiting someone.
- **get back in touch**: communicate with someone again after not communicating with them for a while.
- **face-to-face**: close together and facing each other.

### Band 6.5+
- **multilingual**: able to speak more than two languages very well.
- **widely spoken language**: a language that is used by many people.
- **second language**: a language that you speak well but that is not your native language.
- **dialects**: a particular form of a language which is peculiar to a specific region or social group.
- **dubbed**: (of a film) have the original spoken language replaced by another language.
- **subtitled**: (of a film) have captions displayed at the bottom of the screen that translate the dialogue.
- **loanwords**: a word adopted from a foreign language with little or no modification.
- **accents**: a distinctive way of pronouncing a language, especially one associated with a particular country, area, or social class.
- **integration**: the action or process of integrating.
- **predominantly**: mainly; for the most part.
- **admittedly**: used to introduce a concession or admission.
- **all things considered**: taking everything into account.
- **arguably**: it may be argued (used to qualify the statement of an opinion or belief).
- **as luck would have it**: by good (or bad) luck.
- **even more importantly**: used to emphasize that the following point is more significant.
- **ideally**: in a perfect world.
- **undoubtedly**: without doubt; certainly.
- **lingua franca**: a language that is adopted as a common language between speakers whose native languages are different.
- **hold sway**: have power or influence over.
- **backlash**: a strong negative reaction by a large number of people.
- **immersion**: deep mental involvement in something.

## 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS
- Pattern_1: "Anna got a higher score than Mark." (Using comparative forms)
- Pattern_2: "Anita got the highest score (in the group)." (Using superlative forms)
- Pattern_3: "Using a social networking site may be easier than meeting face-to-face, but it is also less personal." (Making comparisons with adjectives)
- Pattern_4: "Texting is much quicker than writing a letter." (Modifying comparisons with 'much')
- Pattern_5: "American films are by far the most popular films in my country." (Modifying superlatives with 'by far')
- Pattern_6: "The more languages you speak, the easier it is to learn a new one." (Using 'the + comparative..., the + comparative...' structure)
- Pattern_7: "Marie's accent is as difficult to understand as Sophie's." (Expressing equality with 'as... as...')
- Pattern_8: "Due to the emergence of China as a leading economic power, Mandarin will undoubtedly become the foreign language of choice in future years." (Using attitude markers like 'undoubtedly')
- Pattern_9: "More than just important, it's considered essential." (Starting with a strong, emphatic opening)
- Pattern_10: "It depends how far you are from your friends. If you are geographically close, you should meet up face-to-face." (Using 'It depends' to introduce a nuanced answer)

topic_name: "Science and technology"
target_levels: ["5.0-6.0", "6.5-7.5"]

# TOPIC: Hobbies and free time

## 1. PERSONA POOL
- [P1] **Passionate Collector/Hobbyist**: Someone deeply invested in a specific hobby (e.g., photography, art, playing an instrument). They dedicate significant time and resources to it and find it highly rewarding.
- [P2] **Casual Participant**: Has a variety of interests (reading, cooking, occasional sports) but isn't overly obsessed with any single one. Values hobbies as a way to unwind and socialize.
- [P3] **Busy Professional/Parent**: Has limited free time and prefers relaxing, low-effort activities when they do have a moment to spare. Might feel guilty about not doing more "constructive" hobbies.
- [P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

## 2. QUESTION POOL
### Band 5.0 - 6.0
- Q_5_01: What are your hobbies?
- Q_5_02: What is your favourite musical instrument?
- Q_5_03: Do you prefer action films or comedies? Why?
- Q_5_04: Do you think it is important to read novels and poetry? Why?
- Q_5_05: Describe a hobby you enjoy.

### Band 6.5+
- Q_7_01: Do you think men and women tend to have different types of hobbies?
- Q_7_02: Why do some people get obsessed with their hobby?
- Q_7_03: Do you think hobbies that keep you fit are better than hobbies that you can do sitting down?
- Q_7_04: Do you think it can be a disadvantage to have too much free time?
- Q_7_05: Should people feel a duty to do something constructive in their free time?
- Q_7_06: Do people have more free time now than in the past?

## 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL
### Band 5.0 - 6.0
- **keen on**: interested in or attracted by (someone or something).
- **can't stand**: to hate; to be unable to tolerate.
- **crazy about**: very enthusiastic about.
- **don't mind**: to not be annoyed or bothered by something.
- **take it or leave it**: used to say that you do not care whether you have something or not.
- **not my kind of thing**: not something I enjoy or am interested in.
- **camping**: the activity of spending a holiday living in a tent.
- **eating out**: having a meal in a restaurant rather than at home.
- **going clubbing**: going out to nightclubs.
- **page-turner**: a book that is so exciting that you want to read it quickly.
- **abstract**: (of art) not representing outward appearances but expressing ideas or emotions through shapes, colours, etc.
- **exhibition**: a public display of works of art or items of interest.
- **gigs**: live musical performances.
- **rom-com**: romantic comedy.
- **predictable**: always behaving or occurring in the way expected.

### Band 6.5+
- **fanatical about**: obsessively concerned with something.
- **set in**: situated or placed in a particular location or time (especially of a story).
- **thought-provoking**: stimulating careful consideration or attention.
- **hype**: extravagant or intensive publicity or promotion.
- **representational**: (of art) depicting things as they appear in real life.
- **lyrics**: the words of a song.
- **catchy tunes**: a tune that is pleasing and easy to remember.
- **genre**: a style or category of art, music, or literature.
- **star-studded**: featuring a number of famous people.
- **rave reviews**: very enthusiastic reviews or critiques.
- **every now and again**: occasionally.
- **obsessed with**: preoccupy or fill the mind of (someone) continually and to a troubling extent.
- **sedentary**: tending to spend much time seated; somewhat inactive.
- **constructive**: having or intended to have a useful or beneficial purpose.
- **idle hands**: people who have nothing to do.
- **lethargic**: affected by lethargy; sluggish and apathetic.
- **downtime**: time during which one is not working or active; free time.
- **attention spans**: the amount of time someone can concentrate on a task without becoming distracted.
- **labour-saving devices**: machines that reduce the amount of physical effort required to do a task.

## 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS
- Pattern_1: "I've played the clarinet since I was a child." (Present perfect simple for past action continuing to present)
- Pattern_2: "I've been playing the clarinet since I was a child." (Present perfect continuous emphasizing duration)
- Pattern_3: "I've only been scuba-diving twice." (Present perfect simple for experience)
- Pattern_4: "I've been watching TV all morning. I'm so lazy!" (Present perfect continuous focusing on the activity and its present result/feeling)
- Pattern_5: "Yes, I do. The men I know have sports as hobbies. The women usually enjoy more sedentary and peaceful hobbies..." (Answering a general question with a nuanced view)
- Pattern_6: "Having said that, there are of course women who love exhilarating hobbies..." (Using 'Having said that' to introduce an exception or counter-point)
- Pattern_7: "Well, they say that the devil makes work for idle hands and I think it's true that the less you have to do the less active you become..." (Using an idiom and a comparative structure)
- Pattern_8: "People who are always on the go, on the other hand, think nothing of fitting one more thing into their busy schedules." (Using 'on the other hand' for contrast)
- Pattern_9: "It's a strange irony that although we now have so many labour-saving devices... we feel we have less free time." (Using 'It's a strange irony that' to introduce a paradoxical observation)
- Pattern_10: "No, not necessarily. Everyone deserves some downtime." (Disagreeing mildly and offering an alternative perspective)
