topic_id: "youth-and-childhood"
topic_name: "Youth and childhood"
target_levels: ["5.0-6.0", "6.5-7.5"]
---

---

# TOPIC: Youth and childhood

## 1. PERSONA POOL
- [P1] **Nostalgic Adult**: Looks back fondly on their childhood, remembering it as a simpler, happier time. Enjoys reminiscing and feels a strong connection to childhood friends and memories.
- [P2] **Modern Parent**: Focused on current child-rearing practices, values open communication with children (vs. "seen and not heard"), and thinks deeply about the influence of environment and media on child development.
- [P3] **Pragmatic Analyst**: Takes an objective view on childhood, recognizing that it's often idealized. They believe that while upbringing is important, adult choices ultimately define a person.
- [P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

## 2. QUESTION POOL
### Band 5.0 - 6.0
- Q_5_01: What is your most vivid childhood memory?
- Q_5_02: Are you still in touch with your childhood friends?
- Q_5_03: What was your favourite toy when you were a child?
- Q_5_04: Is it important for children to have fun? Why?
- Q_5_05: Describe a happy childhood memory.

### Band 6.5+
- Q_7_01: Do you think people often idealise their childhoods?
- Q_7_02: How does a person's childhood influence what kind of adult they become?
- Q_7_03: When does a child become an adult in your view?
- Q_7_04: Do you agree with the saying 'children should be seen and not heard'?
- Q_7_05: Is it good for children to be exposed to frightening and sad experiences or should they be protected from these as far as possible?
- Q_7_06: Are children in your country generally well brought up?

## 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL
### Band 5.0 - 6.0
- **takes me back**: reminds me of the past.
- **vivid**: clear, detailed, and bright.
- **lasting impression**: an effect or admiration that continues for a long time.
- **fresh in my mind**: clearly remembered, as if it happened recently.
- **vague**: of uncertain, indefinite, or unclear character or meaning.
- **escapes me**: cannot be remembered.
- **on the tip of my tongue**: almost remembered, but not quite.
- **reminisce**: indulge in enjoyable recollection of past events.
- **good old days**: a past period viewed nostalgically.
- **flooding back**: suddenly remembered very clearly.
- **time fly**: time passes very quickly.
- **tell off**: speak to someone angrily or seriously because they have done something wrong.
- **help around the house**: do some of the housework.

### Band 6.5+
- **rose-tinted glasses**: a positive, optimistic, or idealized view of something.
- **latchkey child**: a child who is at home without adult supervision for some part of the day, especially after school until a parent returns from work.
- **child benefit**: money paid by the state to parents.
- **child labour**: the use of children in industry or business, especially when illegal or considered inhumane.
- **child's play**: a thing that is very easy to do.
- **well brought up**: polite and observing social customs because of how one was raised.
- **upbringing**: the way that your parents treat you and the things that they teach you when you are growing up.
- **parenting**: the activity of bringing up and looking after your child.
- **elders**: people who are older than you, usually someone quite a lot older.
- **idealise**: regard or represent as perfect or better than in reality.
- **nostalgic**: feeling, evoking, or characterized by a sentimental longing for the past.
- **dwell on**: think, speak, or write at length about (especially something that is a source of unhappiness, anxiety, or dissatisfaction).
- **nurture**: care for and encourage the growth or development of.
- **nature**: the basic or inherent features of something, especially when seen as characteristic of it (in this context, genetics).
- **role models**: a person looked to by others as an example to be imitated.
- **grapple with**: struggle to deal with or overcome (a difficulty or challenge).
- **leniently**: in a permissively merciful or tolerant manner.
- **havoc**: widespread destruction or confusion.

## 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS
- Pattern_1: "I remember my granddad often used to take me to school when I was little." (Using 'used to' for past habits)
- Pattern_2: "I used to live quite far from my school..." (Using 'used to' for past states)
- Pattern_3: "Then when we had arrived at school, he would push my bike home again." (Using 'would' for past habits)
- Pattern_4: "One day, I was cycling along happily." (Using past continuous for background action)
- Pattern_5: "Suddenly I looked back and my granddad was nowhere to be seen." (Using past simple for interrupting action)
- Pattern_6: "He had tripped on some loose paving." (Using past perfect to show an action happened before another past action)
- Pattern_7: "Certainly they do. The older we get, the more nostalgic we get about the past. It's only normal." (Using 'The older... the more...' structure and validating a feeling)
- Pattern_8: "Well, I suppose the adult you become is influenced by three main factors: firstly... then... and last but not least..." (Structuring a complex answer logically)
- Pattern_9: "To my mind, of all three, nurture has the greatest impact." (Expressing a personal opinion clearly)
- Pattern_10: "That's a difficult question to answer and all societies grapple with this issue." (Buying time and acknowledging the complexity of a question)

# TOPIC: Home and places

## 1. PERSONA POOL
- [P1] **City Dweller**: Thrives in a fast-paced environment, enjoys cosmopolitan culture, hustle and bustle, and convenience of amenities. They might find the countryside boring.
- [P2] **Country Enthusiast**: Prefers a quiet, peaceful life, enjoys nature, open spaces, and community feel. They might find the city too congested, polluted, and stressful.
- [P3] **Suburbanite/Commuter**: Balances city work with a quieter home life. Values good infrastructure and schools but might complain about commuting times and lack of local excitement.
- [P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

## 2. QUESTION POOL
### Band 5.0 - 6.0
- Q_5_01: Tell me about your hometown.
- Q_5_02: In what ways has your town or city changed since you were a child?
- Q_5_03: How could your town or city be improved?
- Q_5_04: Are there any traffic problems where you live?
- Q_5_05: Describe a place that you enjoy visiting.

### Band 6.5+
- Q_7_01: Is it only children who experience homesickness?
- Q_7_02: Do you think it's better for children to grow up in the city or the country?
- Q_7_03: Why do some people retire to the countryside?
- Q_7_04: Are people from your country patriotic?
- Q_7_05: Why do people often feel proud of where they come from?
- Q_7_06: Does intense patriotism have any disadvantages?

## 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL
### Band 5.0 - 6.0
- **urban regeneration**: the process of improving a city or town, often by rebuilding or restoring areas.
- **derelict**: in a very poor condition as a result of disuse and neglect.
- **remote**: (of a place) situated far from the main centres of population; distant.
- **walking distance**: close enough to be reached by walking.
- **hustle and bustle**: busy and noisy activity.
- **idyllic**: like an idyll; extremely happy, peaceful, or picturesque.
- **pace of life**: the speed at which changes and events occur.
- **high-rise flats**: a building with many storeys.
- **middle of nowhere**: a place that is very remote.
- **backwater**: an isolated or peaceful place.
- **picturesque**: visually attractive, especially in a quaint or pretty style.
- **cosmopolitan**: including or containing people from many different countries.
- **tourist destination**: a place that attracts many tourists.
- **polluted**: contaminated with harmful or poisonous substances.
- **spacious**: having ample space.
- **outskirts**: the outer parts of a town or city.

### Band 6.5+
- **congested**: (of a road or place) so crowded with traffic or people as to hinder or prevent freedom of movement.
- **cramped**: feeling or causing someone to feel uncomfortably confined or hemmed in by lack of space.
- **suburbs**: an outlying district of a city, especially a residential one.
- **detached house**: a house not joined to another house on either side.
- **semi-detached house**: a house joined to another house on one side only.
- **terraced house**: a house built as part of a continuous row in a uniform style.
- **bungalow**: a low house, with a broad front porch, having either no upper floor or upper rooms set in the roof, typically with dormer windows.
- **maisonette**: a set of rooms for living in, typically on two storeys of a larger building and having a separate entrance.
- **escalated**: increase rapidly.
- **vibrant**: full of energy and enthusiasm.
- **hive of activity**: a place where a lot of people are working very hard.
- **bustling**: (of a place) full of activity.
- **poignancy**: the quality of evoking a keen sense of sadness or regret.
- **homesickness**: the distress or impairment caused by an actual or anticipated separation from home.
- **culture shock**: the feeling of disorientation experienced by someone when they are suddenly subjected to an unfamiliar culture, way of life, or set of attitudes.
- **patriotic**: having or expressing devotion to and vigorous support for one's country.
- **xenophobia**: intense or irrational dislike or fear of people from other countries.
- **status quo**: the existing state of affairs, especially regarding social or political issues.

## 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS
- Pattern_1: "It is often argued that communities are breaking down." (Using passive voice to introduce a general opinion)
- Pattern_2: "Saint Petersburg is considered by many to be Russia's most beautiful city." (Using passive voice to express a common view)
- Pattern_3: "It has been conclusively shown that children benefit from a stable home environment." (Using passive voice with a 'that' clause for formal statements)
- Pattern_4: "Although the city's National Gallery is often thought to be the most interesting gallery, I prefer the Modern Art Gallery." (Contrasting a general opinion with a personal one)
- Pattern_5: "It could be argued that cities are dangerous places, but I think that if you're streetwise that needn't be the case." (Acknowledging a counter-argument before giving your own view)
- Pattern_6: "I got made redundant last month." (Using 'get' passive for misfortune)
- Pattern_7: "I got accepted into university." (Using 'get' passive for an achievement)
- Pattern_8: "I'm having my hair cut next week." (Using the causative 'have something done')
- Pattern_9: "Patriotism is a terrible thing. Let me rephrase that: patriotism is not always a force for good." (Using 'Let me rephrase that' to self-correct and clarify)
- Pattern_10: "My house is too small. What I mean is that it is too small for our family..." (Using 'What I mean is' to elaborate on a point)

# TOPIC: Holidays and travel

## 1. PERSONA POOL
- [P1] **Adventure Seeker**: Loves backpacking, extreme sports, and going "off the beaten track". Dislikes organized tours and prefers roughing it to experience local cultures authentically.
- [P2] **Comfort Traveler**: Enjoys package holidays, staying in nice hotels, sunbathing, and eating out. Values relaxation and convenience over adventure.
- [P3] **Cultural Explorer**: Travels to visit museums, galleries, and historical sites. Enjoys sightseeing and learning about the history and traditions of different places.
- [P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

## 2. QUESTION POOL
### Band 5.0 - 6.0
- Q_5_01: Is your country popular with tourists?
- Q_5_02: What sights and activities would you recommend to a tourist visiting your town or region?
- Q_5_03: Do you enjoy active holidays?
- Q_5_04: Tell me what your ideal holiday would be.
- Q_5_05: Describe a memorable holiday.

### Band 6.5+
- Q_7_01: Do you think it's true that travel broadens the mind?
- Q_7_02: Do young people and older people benefit differently from travelling?
- Q_7_03: How can you make sure you get the most from your travels?
- Q_7_04: What are the positive impacts of tourism?
- Q_7_05: What about the negative impacts of tourism?
- Q_7_06: How has tourism impacted tourist sites in your country?

## 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL
### Band 5.0 - 6.0
- **package deal**: a holiday organized by a travel agent, with arrangements for transport, accommodation, etc., made at an inclusive price.
- **backpacking**: travelling or camping with your clothes and belongings in a backpack.
- **roughing it**: living in conditions that are not very comfortable or having very little money.
- **self-catering**: (of a holiday or accommodation) offering facilities for people to cook their own meals.
- **sightseeing**: the activity of visiting places of interest in a particular location.
- **excursions**: a short journey or trip, especially one taken as a leisure activity.
- **sunbathing**: sitting or lying in the sun, especially to tan the skin.
- **brochure**: a small book or magazine containing pictures and information about a product or service.
- **camping**: the activity of spending a holiday living in a tent.
- **suitcase**: a case with a handle and a hinged lid, used for carrying clothes and other personal possessions.
- **port**: a town or city with a harbour or access to navigable water where ships load or unload.

### Band 6.5+
- **off the beaten track**: in or into an isolated place.
- **infrastructure**: the basic physical and organizational structures and facilities (e.g. buildings, roads, power supplies) needed for the operation of a society or enterprise.
- **seasonal**: fluctuating or restricted according to the season or time of year.
- **erosion**: the gradual destruction or diminution of something.
- **unspoilt**: (of a place) not marred by development.
- **trade**: the action of buying and selling goods and services.
- **disposable income**: income remaining after deduction of taxes and social security charges, available to be spent or saved as one wishes.
- **low-cost airlines**: an airline that offers generally low fares in exchange for eliminating many traditional passenger services.
- **striking**: (of employees) refuse to work as a form of organized protest.
- **seasickness**: nausea caused by the motion of a ship at sea.
- **volcanic eruption**: the sudden occurrence of a violent discharge of steam and volcanic material.
- **broadens the mind**: helps you understand the world and makes you more accepting of other people's ideas and beliefs.
- **disparage**: represent as being of little worth.
- **manifold**: many and various.
- **detrimental effect**: harmful or damaging effect.
- **drastic step**: an extreme and sudden action.
- **clientele**: clients collectively.

## 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS
- Pattern_1: "Yes, I do." / "No, I don't." (Using short answers correctly to avoid sounding abrupt)
- Pattern_2: "I didn't, no." / "No, she hasn't." (Using contracted forms for negative short answers)
- Pattern_3: "I'm not sure I follow you. - I'll explain it again." (Using 'will' for spontaneous offers or decisions)
- Pattern_4: "People have more disposable income than previous generations, which means that most people can now afford to go on holiday." (Using relative clauses to link cause and effect)
- Pattern_5: "The rapid rise in air travel stems from the creation of low-cost airlines." (Using 'stems from' to explain a cause)
- Pattern_6: "Competition between airlines has led to lower prices for passengers." (Using 'has led to' to explain an effect)
- Pattern_7: "Staff working for our national airline keep striking. As a result, many people avoid booking with them." (Using 'As a result' to connect two sentences)
- Pattern_8: "I suffer from seasickness so I never travel by boat." (Using 'so' to link a cause and a result)
- Pattern_9: "The severe delays were brought about by the volcanic eruption." (Using passive voice and 'brought about by' to explain a cause)
- Pattern_10: "I do think it is unfair that we visit these countries without a thought for what impact tourism has on the local population." (Using 'do' for extra emphasis)

# TOPIC: Children and families

## 1. PERSONA POOL

- [P1] **Speaking Coach**: Supportive IELTS tutor helping the learner develop topic vocabulary and structured responses about children and families.
- [P2] **Academic Discussion Partner**: Analytical conversation partner who asks for causes, effects, comparisons, benefits, drawbacks, and solutions.
- [P3] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

## 2. QUESTION POOL

### Band 5.0 - 6.0

- Q_5_01: The source does not provide a distinct simple Part 1/2 question for this topic; use the topic vocabulary in short personal responses.

### Band 6.5+

- Q_7_01: Some people believe that children should spend all of their free time with their families. Others believe that this is unnecessary or even negative. Discuss the possible arguments on both sides, and say which side you personally support.

## 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

### Band 5.0 - 6.0

- **foster parents**: people who care for children in their family for a fixed period.
- **guardians**: people legally appointed to protect a child's interests.
- **role models**: people children respect as good examples.
- **ground rules**: basic rules governing behaviour in a situation.
- **conventions**: traditions or social norms that people commonly follow.
- **codes of conduct**: rules that people accept in a particular situation.
- **bullying**: attacking or intimidating other children.
- **truancy**: leaving school without permission.

### Band 6.5+

- **delinquency**: minor crime, often committed by young people.
- **abduction**: the crime of taking or kidnapping someone.
- **peer pressure**: pressure from people in the same group to act in a certain way.
- **dual-income**: describing a family where both parents work.
- **breadwinner**: the person who earns all or most of a family's money.
- **child-minding**: informal childcare while parents are busy or working.
- **after-school childcare**: childcare provided in a school setting after normal lessons.
- **viable**: practical and possible to achieve.
- **peers**: people in the same group or at the same level.
- **bonding**: development of close emotional connections.
- **patterns of behaviour**: ways of acting or doing things.
- **well brought-up**: raised with appropriate moral and behavioural conventions.

## 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: “On the one hand, [view/reason]. On the other hand, [contrasting view/reason].”
- Pattern_2: “Furthermore, [additional point], leading to [result].”
- Pattern_3: “Conversely, [contrasting point].”
- Pattern_4: “Overall, it seems that [balanced conclusion].”
- Pattern_5: “Perhaps the main factor is [cause].”
- Pattern_6: “Regarding potential solutions, probably the main remedy would be to [solution].”
- Pattern_7: “A further cause seems to be [cause].”
- Pattern_8: “Another solution may be to [solution].”
- Pattern_9: “There can be few choices in life more important than [topic].”
- Pattern_10: “It must be admitted that [concession]. Nevertheless, [main position].”
- Pattern_11: “It is true that [concession]. However, [qualification/contrast].”
- Pattern_12: “To conclude, it appears that [summary].”

# TOPIC: Global challenges

## 1. PERSONA POOL

- [P1] **Speaking Coach**: Supportive IELTS tutor helping the learner develop topic vocabulary and structured responses about global challenges.
- [P2] **Academic Discussion Partner**: Analytical conversation partner who asks for causes, effects, comparisons, benefits, drawbacks, and solutions.
- [P3] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

## 2. QUESTION POOL

### Band 5.0 - 6.0

- Q_5_01: The source does not provide a distinct simple Part 1/2 question for this topic; use the topic vocabulary in short personal responses.

### Band 6.5+

- Q_7_01: Unemployment remains the biggest challenge to school-leavers in most countries. How far do you agree with this assessment? What other challenges face young people today?

## 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

### Band 5.0 - 6.0

- **a challenge**: a problem or difficulty to be faced.
- **economic or political instability**: rapid, unmanaged change in a country's economy or political situation.
- **social unrest**: riots, protests or conflict involving the public.
- **an issue**: a topic, subject or question that must be considered.
- **pressing matters**: urgent and important issues.
- **joblessness**: unemployment.
- **economic prospects**: future possibilities for an economy.
- **social mobility**: the ability to progress in salary, lifestyle and social status.

### Band 6.5+

- **the private sector**: private businesses rather than state-controlled services.
- **demographics**: changes in society involving age, income, numbers, origins and location.
- **longevity**: tendency to have a long life expectancy.
- **to migrate**: to move permanently between countries.
- **brain drain**: migration of skilled or successful workers out of a country.
- **existential threats**: threats to existence or life.
- **an outbreak**: the start of a widespread problem such as disease or conflict.
- **civil war**: war between people inside a country.
- **livelihood**: the way a person makes a living.
- **developed countries**: countries with advanced economies, industries and infrastructure.
- **developing countries**: countries not yet considered fully developed.

## 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: “On the one hand, [view/reason]. On the other hand, [contrasting view/reason].”
- Pattern_2: “Furthermore, [additional point], leading to [result].”
- Pattern_3: “Conversely, [contrasting point].”
- Pattern_4: “Overall, it seems that [balanced conclusion].”
- Pattern_5: “Perhaps the main factor is [cause].”
- Pattern_6: “Regarding potential solutions, probably the main remedy would be to [solution].”
- Pattern_7: “A further cause seems to be [cause].”
- Pattern_8: “Another solution may be to [solution].”
- Pattern_9: “There can be few choices in life more important than [topic].”
- Pattern_10: “It must be admitted that [concession]. Nevertheless, [main position].”
- Pattern_11: “It is true that [concession]. However, [qualification/contrast].”
- Pattern_12: “To conclude, it appears that [summary].”

# TOPIC: Cities and infrastructure

## 1. PERSONA POOL

- [P1] **Speaking Coach**: Supportive IELTS tutor helping the learner develop topic vocabulary and structured responses about cities and infrastructure.
- [P2] **Academic Discussion Partner**: Analytical conversation partner who asks for causes, effects, comparisons, benefits, drawbacks, and solutions.
- [P3] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

## 2. QUESTION POOL

### Band 5.0 - 6.0

- Q_5_01: The source does not provide a distinct simple Part 1/2 question for this topic; use the topic vocabulary in short personal responses.

### Band 6.5+

- Q_7_01: Transport delays and long journey times are a widespread phenomenon in many cities today. What are the causes of this problem, and how could the situation be improved?

## 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

### Band 5.0 - 6.0

- **urban**: related to cities.
- **investment or funding**: money provided to pay for an activity.
- **infrastructure**: physical and system organisation supporting a city, area or country.
- **high-capacity**: able to handle high volumes of people or goods.
- **public transport**: state-funded transport such as buses and trains.
- **congestion**: too much traffic causing delays.
- **conurbations**: very large cities that have absorbed other towns.
- **transport hubs**: centres where many transport routes converge.

### Band 6.5+

- **facilities and amenities**: places providing services to the public.
- **a bottleneck**: a place where congestion regularly occurs.
- **overcrowding**: a situation where too many people live in one place.
- **hinterland**: the area around a city affected by its development.
- **to settle in a place**: to move and live there permanently.
- **urban sprawl**: uncontrolled expansion of a city.
- **public-private partnerships**: projects jointly funded by government and business.
- **civic pride**: pride in the town or city where one lives.
- **tax incentives**: tax reductions used to encourage an activity.
- **home working**: working from home for all or part of the week.
- **to commute**: to travel a long distance to work regularly.
- **suburbs**: residential areas around a city.
- **the inner city**: the older central part of a city.
- **communal car-pooling**: shared-car travel intended to reduce fuel use.
- **outdated**: old-fashioned and no longer relevant.
- **innovations**: new ideas or things that are useful or exciting.
- **a burden**: a responsibility or weight that is difficult to cope with.

## 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: “On the one hand, [view/reason]. On the other hand, [contrasting view/reason].”
- Pattern_2: “Furthermore, [additional point], leading to [result].”
- Pattern_3: “Conversely, [contrasting point].”
- Pattern_4: “Overall, it seems that [balanced conclusion].”
- Pattern_5: “Perhaps the main factor is [cause].”
- Pattern_6: “Regarding potential solutions, probably the main remedy would be to [solution].”
- Pattern_7: “A further cause seems to be [cause].”
- Pattern_8: “Another solution may be to [solution].”
- Pattern_9: “There can be few choices in life more important than [topic].”
- Pattern_10: “It must be admitted that [concession]. Nevertheless, [main position].”
- Pattern_11: “It is true that [concession]. However, [qualification/contrast].”
- Pattern_12: “To conclude, it appears that [summary].”

# TOPIC: Government and the authorities

## 1. PERSONA POOL

- [P1] **Speaking Coach**: Supportive IELTS tutor helping the learner develop topic vocabulary and structured responses about government and the authorities.
- [P2] **Academic Discussion Partner**: Analytical conversation partner who asks for causes, effects, comparisons, benefits, drawbacks, and solutions.
- [P3] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

## 2. QUESTION POOL

### Band 5.0 - 6.0

- Q_5_01: The source does not provide a distinct simple Part 1/2 question for this topic; use the topic vocabulary in short personal responses.

### Band 6.5+

- Q_7_01: Some observers say that police officers should be recruited from the communities where they work, so that they have local knowledge. Other people say that this is unnecessary, or even undesirable. Where do you stand on this debate? Is local knowledge essential in modern policing?

## 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

### Band 5.0 - 6.0

- **trustworthy**: capable of being trusted.
- **paramount**: of the greatest importance.
- **devious**: extremely clever in a dishonest way.
- **minutiae**: small details.
- **grievance**: an issue that makes people upset or angry for a long time.
- **sensitivity**: awareness of the circumstances of a specific group.
- **to commit an offence / a crime**: to carry out an offence or crime.
- **informants**: people who provide police with useful information.
- **evidence**: material presented in court to prove guilt or innocence.
- **a trial**: the legal procedure for prosecuting someone for a crime.

### Band 6.5+

- **conviction rates**: the percentage of accused people who are found guilty.
- **a deterrent**: something that discourages people from doing something.
- **sentencing**: the act of deciding or announcing a criminal punishment.
- **imprisonment**: punishment by being kept in prison.
- **a fine**: money paid as a punishment.
- **community service**: punishment involving work for the public.
- **rehabilitation**: the process of changing a criminal's behaviour to prevent further crime.
- **an offender**: a person who commits an offence.
- **tendencies**: inclinations associated with a person's character.
- **corruption**: illegal behaviour by officials for personal or connected interests.
- **bribery**: giving money or benefits to officials to obtain something.
- **to enforce laws**: to apply laws to people.
- **unequitable**: unfair or unequal between groups.
- **law-abiding**: following laws properly.
- **transferable skills**: skills usable in different situations.
- **lateral thinking**: creative thinking using new approaches.
- **investigative**: related to investigating causes of crime or problems.
- **to transcend a situation**: to go beyond a specific situation or context.
- **a context**: a specific situation.
- **to outweigh**: to be more important than.
- **over-familiarity**: being excessively friendly with the public in an official role.

## 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: “On the one hand, [view/reason]. On the other hand, [contrasting view/reason].”
- Pattern_2: “Furthermore, [additional point], leading to [result].”
- Pattern_3: “Conversely, [contrasting point].”
- Pattern_4: “Overall, it seems that [balanced conclusion].”
- Pattern_5: “Perhaps the main factor is [cause].”
- Pattern_6: “Regarding potential solutions, probably the main remedy would be to [solution].”
- Pattern_7: “A further cause seems to be [cause].”
- Pattern_8: “Another solution may be to [solution].”
- Pattern_9: “There can be few choices in life more important than [topic].”
- Pattern_10: “It must be admitted that [concession]. Nevertheless, [main position].”
- Pattern_11: “It is true that [concession]. However, [qualification/contrast].”
- Pattern_12: “To conclude, it appears that [summary].”

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: Do you have a large family?

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

- `an only child`: a person with no brothers or sisters.
- `bossy`: telling other people what to do.
- `calm`: quiet and not showing worry or excitement.
- `clever`: intelligent and quick to understand.
- `confident`: sure of your own abilities.
- `creative`: able to invent and develop original ideas.
- `friendly`: behaving in a kind and pleasant way.
- `funny`: causing amusement or laughter.
- `kind`: considerate and generous to others.
- `look like`: have the same appearance as.
- `curly hair`: hair with curls.

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

# TOPIC: FREE TIME

#### 1. PERSONA POOL

[P1] **Active Person**: Enjoys physical activities and exercise in their free time.
[P2] **Relaxation Seeker**: Prefers relaxing activities like reading or doing nothing.
[P3] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

#### 2. QUESTION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: What do you like doing in your free time?
- Q_5_02: What is your favourite thing to do?
- Q_5_03: Do you like playing computer games?
- Q_5_04: Do you like reading magazines?
- Q_5_05: When do you usually do these activities?

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `chatting online`: talking to people on the internet.
- `doing nothing`: relaxing without doing any specific activity.
- `going for walks`: walking for pleasure.
- `going shopping`: visiting shops to buy things.
- `playing computer games`: using a computer for games.
- `reading magazines`: looking at periodicals.
- `watching a film`: viewing a movie.
- `boring`: dull and lacking interest.
- `exciting`: making you feel happy and enthusiastic.
- `interesting`: making you want to know more.
- `relaxing`: making you feel calm.

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: "My favourite thing is [verb-ing]."

- Pattern_3: "I prefer [verb-ing] to [verb-ing]."

# TOPIC: SPECIAL OCCASIONS

#### 1. PERSONA POOL

[P1] **Celebrant**: Enjoys festivals and family occasions, describes traditions and activities.
[P2] **Traveler**: Likes to experience different celebrations in other countries.
[P3] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

#### 2. QUESTION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: What is an important festival in your country?
- Q_5_02: How do you celebrate this festival?
- Q_5_03: What are your favourite parts of this festival?
- Q_5_04: Have you ever been to a special occasion in another country?
- Q_5_05: Do you think festivals are important for a society?

**Band 6.5+**
- Q_7_01: Describe a special occasion in your life.
- Q_7_02: How have special occasions such as weddings changed in your country?
- Q_7_03: What is the importance of festivals in a society?
- Q_7_04: Should we preserve traditional celebrations?

#### 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `carnival`: a public festival with music and dancing.
- `costume`: a set of clothes worn by an actor or for a festival.
- `parade`: a public procession.
- `fireworks`: small containers that explode and produce coloured sparks.
- `traditional food`: food that is typical of a country or culture.
- `special occasion`: an important event like a wedding or festival.
- `gave presents`: offered gifts.

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: "In my country, we celebrate [occasion]. We [verb] and [verb]."
- Pattern_2: "Last year, I went to [event]. It was [adjective]."
- Pattern_3: "When we saw the [event], it was very exciting! We [verb] and [verb]."

# TOPIC: HOMETOWN

- Q_7_03: How has your hometown changed?

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `busy city`: a city with lots of traffic and activity.
- `near the beach`: close to the sea.
- `famous for`: well-known for something.
- `friendly people`: nice and helpful residents.
- `traffic`: vehicles on the road.
- `neighbourhoods`: areas of a city.
- `harbour`: a place on the coast where ships dock.
- `museum`: a building where objects of historical or artistic interest are displayed.
- `shopping mall`: a large indoor shopping centre.
- `tall buildings`: skyscrapers.

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

- Pattern_3: "I like it because the people are very [adjective]."

# TOPIC: TV AND RADIO

#### 1. PERSONA POOL

[P1] **TV Enthusiast**: Has a favourite show, describes characters and plots.
[P2] **News Viewer**: Stays informed and watches the news or documentaries.
[P3] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

#### 2. QUESTION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: What do you usually watch on television?
- Q_5_02: What are some popular programmes in your country?
- Q_5_03: Why do you think some TV programmes are popular?
- Q_5_04: When did you last watch television?
- Q_5_05: What type of programmes do you enjoy?

**Band 6.5+**
- Q_7_01: Talk about a programme you learned something from.
- Q_7_02: Talk about a TV programme you enjoy watching.
- Q_7_03: How has TV changed in your country?
- Q_7_04: Do you think people watch too much TV?

#### 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `documentary`: a factual programme.
- `game show`: a programme where contestants win prizes.
- `news`: a programme about current events.
- `soap opera`: a continuing drama about people's lives.
- `talk show`: a programme where guests are interviewed.
- `presenter`: the person who hosts a programme.
- `contestants`: people taking part in a game show.
- `characters`: people in a story.
- `actors`: people who play characters.
- `drama series`: a fictional programme with a continuing story.

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: "I always/often/sometimes/never watch [type of programme]."
- Pattern_2: "It's presented by [person] and it stars [actors]."
- Pattern_3: "I watch it [time/frequency] because I think it's [adjective]."

# TOPIC: HOLIDAYS AND TRAVEL

#### 1. PERSONA POOL

[P1] **Adventurous Traveler**: Loves exploring new places and trying new activities.
[P2] **Relaxing Tourist**: Prefers relaxing holidays like beach holidays.
[P3] **Culture Seeker**: Enjoys visiting historical sites and experiencing traditional food.
[P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

#### 2. QUESTION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: Do you like travelling? Why/Why not?
- Q_5_02: When was your last holiday?
- Q_5_03: What kinds of places do you like visiting?
- Q_5_04: Is there a country you would like to visit?
- Q_5_05: Do you think tourists enjoy visiting your country?

**Band 6.5+**
- Q_7_01: Talk about a special holiday or trip.
- Q_7_02: Compare your experience of holidays and travelling with your parents' generation.
- Q_7_03: Many people think it is a good idea to go on holiday in your own country. Do you agree?
- Q_7_04: In your opinion, is it important to speak the language of the country you're visiting?

#### 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `beach holiday`: a holiday relaxing on a beach.
- `activity holiday`: a holiday doing sports and physical activities.
- `coach tour`: a tour of an area by bus.
- `safari`: an expedition to observe wild animals.
- `go sightseeing`: to visit tourist attractions.
- `go on day trips`: to visit a place and come back on the same day.
- `learn water sports`: to take up sports in the water.
- `visit local festivals`: to go to festivals in the area.
- `eat in traditional restaurants`: to dine in authentic local places.

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: "Last year, I went on a [type] holiday to [place]. It was [adjective]."
- Pattern_2: "I'm going to [verb] and I'm not going to [verb]."
- Pattern_3: "I prefer [type of travel] because it's [adjective]."

# TOPIC: IMPORTANT EVENTS

#### 1. PERSONA POOL

[P1] **Reflective Person**: Talks about past important events and feelings.
[P2] **Achiever**: Describes events like graduation or winning competitions.
[P3] **Cultural Observer**: Discusses the significance of important events in their culture.
[P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

#### 2. QUESTION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: Did you have a favourite teacher at school?
- Q_5_02: What's your experience of travelling to other countries?
- Q_5_03: Do you come from a large family?
- Q_5_04: Describe your hometown.
- Q_5_05: When was your last holiday?

**Band 6.5+**
- Q_7_01: Describe an important event in your life.
- Q_7_02: Compare how important events, like weddings, have changed compared to your parents' generation.
- Q_7_03: In your opinion, how should people remember important events from their country's past?
- Q_7_04: Many people think photos are the best way to remember special occasions. Do you agree?

#### 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `coming of age`: reaching adulthood.
- `getting married`: the ceremony of marriage.
- `getting your first job`: starting employment.
- `graduating from university`: completing a degree.
- `travelling on your own for the first time`: going alone.
- `winning a sports competition`: coming first in a contest.
- `nervous`: worried or anxious.
- `excited`: happy and enthusiastic.
- `surprised`: having a feeling of astonishment.
- `delighted`: extremely pleased.
- `satisfied`: happy with the result.

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: "I think the most important event for a student is [event]."
- Pattern_2: "It is more important than [event] because [reason]."
- Pattern_3: "I was very [adjective] when [event] happened."
- Pattern_4: "I felt [adjective] because [reason]."

# TOPIC: POSSESSIONS

#### 1. PERSONA POOL

[P1] **Sentimental Owner**: Values objects with emotional significance, like a gift or heirloom.
[P2] **Practical User**: Focuses on the utility and functionality of their possessions.
[P3] **Consumer**: Enjoys shopping and buying new things.
[P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

#### 2. QUESTION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: Which country are you from?
- Q_5_02: Which is the most popular place to go shopping in your hometown?
- Q_5_03: How often do you shop online?
- Q_5_04: When did you last buy a present for someone?
- Q_5_05: What do you like doing when you go out?

**Band 6.5+**
- Q_7_01: Describe something you own which is very important to you.
- Q_7_02: In your opinion, do possessions make people happy?
- Q_7_03: Compare the important possessions you have with the important possessions your parents have.
- Q_7_04: Many people think that shopping is a good way to relax. Do you agree?

#### 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `large`: much bigger than average.
- `small`: not large.
- `modern`: relating to the present time.
- `new`: recently made.
- `old-fashioned`: no longer fashionable.
- `black`: the darkest colour.
- `green`: a colour between yellow and blue.
- `red`: the colour of blood.
- `glass`: a hard, transparent substance.
- `leather`: the tanned skin of animals.
- `metal`: a chemical element like iron or copper.
- `plastic`: a substance made by a chemical process.
- `wood`: the substance that forms trees.

**Band 6.5+**
- (None available in source material)

#### 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: "One of my favourite possessions is my [object]."
- Pattern_2: "It's made of [material] and it's [size, colour]."
- Pattern_3: "I've had it for [time] and it reminds me of [person/place]."
- Pattern_4: "It's important to me because [reason]."

---

# TOPIC: ACCOMMODATION & BUILDINGS

#### 1. PERSONA POOL

[P1] **Urban Dweller**: Lives in a modern apartment in the city, values convenience and amenities.
[P2] **Suburban Resident**: Prefers a house with a garden, values space and peace.
[P3] **Architecture Enthusiast**: Interested in different types of buildings, their history and design.
[P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

#### 2. QUESTION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: Do you live in a house or an apartment?
- Q_5_02: What do you like about your home?
- Q_5_03: Would you like to move to a different home in the future?
- Q_5_04: What kind of building do you live in?

**Band 6.5+**
- Q_7_01: Describe a building you like in your city.
- Q_7_02: What are the advantages and disadvantages of living in a house versus an apartment?
- Q_7_03: How have buildings in your country changed in recent decades?
- Q_7_04: Do you think modern buildings are more functional or more attractive?

#### 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `small but comfortable`: not large, but pleasant.
- `located near my workplace`: close to where I work.
- `cozy and quiet`: comfortable and peaceful.
- `safe neighborhood`: a secure area.
- `nice view from the balcony`: a good outlook from the balcony.
- `a great place to relax`: a nice area to unwind.
- `bigger house with a garden`: a larger home with an outdoor space.

**Band 6.5+**
- `high-rise building`: a tall building with many floors.
- `modern amenities`: current and convenient facilities like gyms, pools.
- `spacious and airy`: large and well-ventilated.
- `architectural style`: a particular design or look of a building.
- `blend in with the surroundings`: to fit in with the environment.
- `functional design`: a practical and useful layout.
- `aesthetic appeal`: visual beauty or attractiveness.

#### 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: "I live in a/an [type of home] in [location]. It's [adjective] but [adjective]."
- Pattern_2: "I like that my home is [adjective]. It's a great place to [verb]."
- Pattern_3: "In the future, I'd love to [verb] because [clause]."
- Pattern_4: "What I find most appealing about [building] is [feature]. It was built in [time] and has [features]."

`topic_name: "Cities & Towns"`
`target_levels: ["5.0-6.0", "6.5-7.5"]`

##

---

# TOPIC: CITIES & TOWNS

#### 1. PERSONA POOL

[P1] **City Lover**: Enjoys the fast pace, entertainment, and opportunities of urban life.
[P2] **Small-Town Resident**: Appreciates the peace, community, and slower pace of small towns.
[P3] **Tourist/Visitor**: Observes and compares different cities and towns, talks about attractions.
[P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

#### 2. QUESTION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: Where is your hometown?
- Q_5_02: What do you like about your city/town?
- Q_5_03: Is your city/town a good place to live?
- Q_5_04: Have you lived in other cities?

**Band 6.5+**
- Q_7_01: Describe a city you have visited that impressed you.
- Q_7_02: What are the pros and cons of living in a big city?
- Q_7_03: How is your city/town changing?
- Q_7_04: What can be done to improve your city/town?

#### 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `quiet and peaceful`: calm and tranquil.
- `friendly people`: nice and amiable residents.
- `fresh air`: clean, unpolluted atmosphere.
- `relaxing atmosphere`: a laid-back environment.
- `delicious and affordable food`: good food at reasonable prices.
- `strong sense of community`: a close community spirit.
- `limited job opportunities`: not many jobs available.

**Band 6.5+**
- `cosmopolitan`: containing people from many different countries and cultures.
- `bustling city`: a lively and busy city.
- `a wide array of`: a large variety of.
- `cultural melting pot`: a place where different cultures mix.
- `traffic congestion`: heavy traffic jams.
- `green spaces`: parks and areas with trees.
- `urban sprawl`: the uncontrolled expansion of a city.

#### 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: "My hometown is [adjective] because it's [adjective] and [adjective]."
- Pattern_2: "I like [noun] because it's [adjective]. Also, [clause]."
- Pattern_3: "The city has a lot to offer, such as [examples]. However, it also suffers from [problems]."
- Pattern_4: "One of the main improvements that could be made is [solution]. This would [benefit]."

# TOPIC: FASHION & SHOPPING

#### 1. PERSONA POOL

[P1] **Style-Conscious Shopper**: Enjoys following trends, uses fashion to express personal style.
[P2] **Comfort-Seeker**: Prioritizes comfort and practicality over trends.
[P3] **Smart Shopper**: Likes to find deals, prefers online shopping for convenience.
[P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

#### 2. QUESTION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: Do you like shopping?
- Q_5_02: Do you prefer shopping online or in stores?
- Q_5_03: What's the last thing you bought?
- Q_5_04: Do you like fashion?

**Band 6.5+**
- Q_7_01: Describe a piece of clothing you like to wear.
- Q_7_02: How has fashion changed in your country?
- Q_7_03: Do you think fashion is important in society?
- Q_7_04: What are the advantages and disadvantages of online shopping?

#### 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `explore different styles`: to try out various fashion looks.
- `find good deals`: to get items at a low price.
- `careful with my spending`: to not spend too much money.
- `compare prices`: to look at costs of different items.
- `read reviews`: to see what other people think of a product.
- `try them on first`: to put on clothes to test the fit.
- `personal development`: activities that improve skills and character.

**Band 6.5+**
- `express one's personality`: to show one's character through style.
- `fast fashion`: cheap, trendy clothing produced quickly.
- `make a statement`: to make an impression or express an opinion.
- `wardrobe staples`: essential clothing items.
- `consumer behavior`: the habits of people buying things.
- `ethical/sustainable fashion`: clothing made with consideration for the environment and workers.

#### 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: "I enjoy shopping, especially for [items]. However, I try to [verb]."
- Pattern_2: "I prefer [method] because it's [adjective]."
- Pattern_3: "The last thing I bought was a [item] about [subject]."
- Pattern_4: "Fashion allows people to [verb]. However, there is also the problem of [issue]."

# TOPIC: SPORTS & ACTIVITIES

#### 1. PERSONA POOL

[P1] **Active Participant**: Enjoys playing sports for fitness, teamwork, and fun.
[P2] **Armchair Fan**: Prefers watching sports, follows teams and tournaments closely.
[P3] **Fit Lifestyle Advocate**: Sees sports as essential for a healthy lifestyle.
[P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

#### 2. QUESTION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: Do you like playing sports?
- Q_5_02: What is your favorite sport?
- Q_5_03: Do you prefer playing or watching sports?
- Q_5_04: What sports are popular in your country?

**Band 6.5+**
- Q_7_01: Describe a sport you would like to try.
- Q_7_02: What are the benefits of playing sports?
- Q_7_03: Should schools encourage students to play sports?
- Q_7_04: How has the way people watch sports changed with technology?

#### 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `stay fit and healthy`: to remain physically well.
- `have fun with friends`: to enjoy time with people.
- `easy to play`: simple to participate in.
- `great way to exercise`: a good method to work out.
- `keeps me active`: maintains my involvement.
- `important matches`: significant games.
- `during big tournaments`: throughout main competitions.

**Band 6.5+**
- `teamwork and communication skills`: ability to work well with others.
- `physically demanding`: requiring a lot of physical effort.
- `mental health benefits`: positive effects on psychological well-being.
- `a sense of camaraderie`: a feeling of friendship and trust within a group.
- `sportsmanship`: fair, generous, and polite behavior in sports.
- `live streaming`: watching events live on the internet.

#### 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: "I like playing sports because it helps me [verb]."
- Pattern_2: "I enjoy playing [sport] because it's [adjective]."
- Pattern_3: "I prefer [verb-ing] because it keeps me [adjective]."
- Pattern_4: "Sports play a vital role because they help people [verb] and [verb]."

# TOPIC: PEOPLE

#### 1. PERSONA POOL

[P1] **Social Friend**: Values close friendships and enjoys spending quality time with people.
[P2] **Family-Oriented Person**: Focuses on family relationships, talks about relatives and traditions.
[P3] **Observant Individual**: Notices and describes personalities and characteristics of people around them.
[P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

#### 2. QUESTION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: Do you have many friends?
- Q_5_02: How often do you meet your friends?
- Q_5_03: What do you usually do with your friends?
- Q_5_04: Can you describe your family?

**Band 6.5+**
- Q_7_01: Describe a person you admire.
- Q_7_02: What qualities make a good friend/parent/leader?
- Q_7_03: How have relationships between people changed in modern society?
- Q_7_04: Do you think people are less social now than in the past?

#### 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `small group of close friends`: a limited number of good friends.
- `quality over quantity`: focusing on the value of things rather than number.
- `real friendship is about trust and understanding`: a true friendship is based on confidence and empathy.
- `at least once a week`: a minimum of one time per week.
- `hang out and talk`: to spend time together and chat.

**Band 6.5+**
- `good listener`: someone who listens attentively.
- `a sense of humor`: the ability to make others laugh.
- `role model`: a person worth emulating.
- `strong work ethic`: a belief in the importance of hard work.
- `dependable and trustworthy`: reliable and honest.
- `shared interests`: common hobbies or passions.
- `socializing with others`: spending time with other people.

#### 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: "I have a [adjective] group of friends. I prefer [noun] over [noun]."
- Pattern_2: "We usually go out for [activity] or just [verb] and [verb]."
- Pattern_3: "Spending time with friends helps me [verb] and [verb]."
- Pattern_4: "The most admirable quality of my [person] is their ability to [verb]."

# TOPIC: SPECIAL EVENTS

#### 1. PERSONA POOL

[P1] **Event-Goer**: Enjoys attending parties, festivals, and celebrations.
[P2] **Family Celebrant**: Values events like weddings and birthdays for bringing family together.
[P3] **Cultural Enthusiast**: Interested in traditional festivals and their significance.
[P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

#### 2. QUESTION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: What's your favorite holiday?
- Q_5_02: How do you celebrate this holiday?
- Q_5_03: What special events do you like?
- Q_5_04: What did you do last weekend?

**Band 6.5+**
- Q_7_01: Describe a special event you attended.
- Q_7_02: What is the importance of festivals in a society?
- Q_7_03: How have celebrations changed over the years?
- Q_7_04: Do you think traditional celebrations are disappearing?

#### 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `enjoyed it a lot`: had a great time.
- `had a great time`: enjoyed themselves.
- `spent time with my family`: used time being with family.
- `ate delicious food`: consumed tasty food.
- `gave presents`: exchanged gifts.
- `had a day off`: had a holiday from work/study.

**Band 6.5+**
- `cultural significance`: importance to a culture.
- `festive atmosphere`: a lively, celebratory mood.
- `bring the community together`: unite the people in a community.
- `traditional customs`: usual practices for an event.
- `family reunion`: a gathering of family members.
- `memorable occasion`: an event worth remembering.
- `adapt to modern times`: to change with the current era.

#### 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: "My favorite event is [event] because it allows me to [verb]."
- Pattern_2: "I had a great time because I could [verb] and [verb]."
- Pattern_3: "On my last birthday, I [verb-ed] and [verb-ed]."
- Pattern_4: "Festivals are important because they serve as a way to [verb]."

# TOPIC: DAILY LIFE

#### 1. PERSONA POOL

[P1] **Busy Worker**: Follows a strict daily routine to manage time efficiently.
[P2] **Relaxed Individual**: Enjoys a flexible schedule, values free time.
[P3] **Morning Person**: Prefers the energy and focus of the morning hours.
[P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

#### 2. QUESTION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: What do you usually do in a day?
- Q_5_02: Do you have a fixed daily routine?
- Q_5_03: What part of the day do you enjoy the most?
- Q_5_04: When do you normally wake up?

**Band 6.5+**
- Q_7_01: Describe a typical day in your life.
- Q_7_02: How has your daily routine changed over the years?
- Q_7_03: Do you think it's better to have a strict routine or a flexible one?
- Q_7_04: What would you like to change in your daily routine?

#### 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `wake up early`: to get up early in the morning.
- `relax by reading books`: to find rest and peace by reading.
- `follow a regular schedule`: to have a set routine.
- `stay organized`: to be well-ordered and structured.
- `make small changes`: to change some minor aspects of a routine.
- `relax after a long day`: to unwind after a tiring day.
- `spend time with`: to use time being with someone.

**Band 6.5+**
- `productive day`: a day where much is achieved.
- `hit the snooze button`: to use the button on an alarm clock to get more sleep.
- `get ready for the day`: to prepare oneself.
- `take a break`: to have a short rest.
- `wind down in the evening`: to relax at the end of the day.
- `keep a routine`: to maintain a schedule.

#### 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: "On weekdays, I [verb] and [verb]. In the evening, I [verb]."
- Pattern_2: "I follow a [adjective] schedule because it helps me [verb]."
- Pattern_3: "I enjoy [time] the most because I can [verb]."
- Pattern_4: "A typical day for me starts with [activity] and ends with [activity]."

## Topic 14: Life Experiences

`topic_id: "life_experiences"`
`topic_name: "Life Experiences"`
`target_levels: ["5.0-6.0", "6.5-7.5"]`

##

---

# TOPIC: LIFE EXPERIENCES

#### 1. PERSONA POOL

[P1] **Reflective Person**: Enjoys telling stories about past experiences and the lessons learned.
[P2] **Adventurer**: Has many exciting experiences, likes to try new things.
[P3] **Learner**: Sees every experience as a chance to grow and learn.
[P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

#### 2. QUESTION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: What was your first day at school like?
- Q_5_02: What is the most memorable thing you have done?
- Q_5_03: Who has influenced you the most in your life?
- Q_5_04: Have you ever had a challenge?

**Band 6.5+**
- Q_7_01: Describe an important experience in your life.
- Q_7_02: What lessons have you learned from your past experiences?
- Q_7_03: Do you think people learn more from successes or failures?
- Q_7_04: How can people gain valuable life experiences?

#### 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `for the first time`: never before.
- `felt nervous and excited`: was both worried and happy.
- `had a great time`: had a lot of fun.
- `never forget`: will always remember.
- `learn a lot from`: to gain much knowledge from.
- `make me stronger`: to become more resilient.
- `useful for my future`: helpful for later life.

**Band 6.5+**
- `a life-changing experience`: an event that significantly changes your life.
- `step out of my comfort zone`: to try new things that are outside of routine.
- `overcome a challenge`: to successfully deal with a difficult situation.
- `gain new perspectives`: to see things from different viewpoints.
- `take risks`: to do something that may have a negative outcome.
- `resilience`: the ability to recover from setbacks.

#### 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: "It was the first time I had ever [verb-ed]. I felt [adjective] but [adjective]."
- Pattern_2: "That event taught me a lot about [topic]. I learned that [clause]."
- Pattern_3: "If I had the chance to change something, I would [verb]."
- Pattern_4: "I believe that failure is an important part of success because it helps us [verb]."

## Topic 15: Education & Learning

`topic_id: "education_learning"`
`topic_name: "Education & Learning"`
`target_levels: ["5.0-6.0", "6.5-7.5"]`

##

---

# TOPIC: COMMUNICATION

#### 1. PERSONA POOL

[P1] **Social Communicator**: Prefers face-to-face interaction, values non-verbal cues.
[P2] **Tech-Savvy Communicator**: Relies on technology for communication, prefers texting.
[P3] **Language Learner**: Discusses the challenges and importance of languages in communication.
[P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

#### 2. QUESTION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- Q_5_01: Do you often talk to people?
- Q_5_02: Do you prefer speaking to people face-to-face or on the phone?
- Q_5_03: What is your favorite way to communicate?
- Q_5_04: Do you think it's important to learn a foreign language?

**Band 6.5+**
- Q_7_01: How has technology affected the way we communicate?
- Q_7_02: Do you think people are better at communicating now than in the past?
- Q_7_03: What are the benefits of being a good communicator?
- Q_7_04: Will face-to-face communication disappear in the future?

#### 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

**Band 5.0 - 6.0**
- `face-to-face communication`: talking to someone in person.
- `on the phone`: talking via telephone.
- `text messages`: written messages sent via phone.
- `keep in touch with`: to maintain contact with.
- `expressing myself clearly`: to speak or write without ambiguity.

**Band 6.5+**
- `non-verbal cues`: physical gestures and facial expressions.
- `body language`: gestures and posture that communicate feelings.
- `the art of conversation`: the skill of talking to others.
- `digital communication`: sending messages via electronic devices.
- `misunderstanding`: failure to understand correctly.
- `bridge the gap`: to connect differences.

#### 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1: "I prefer [method] because it's more [adjective]."
- Pattern_2: "Technology has made communication [adjective] but sometimes [adjective]."
- Pattern_3: "Being a good communicator can help you [verb] and [verb]."
- Pattern_4: "Despite the rise of digital communication, I think [method] will always be important."

# TOPIC: Everyday Transactions — Registration, Enrollment & Customer Service Calls

## 1. PERSONA POOL

- [P1] **Library/Front-Desk Clerk**: Polite, procedural; collects personal details (name, address, phone number, membership number) and explains how a service (club, membership) works.
- [P2] **University Admissions Officer**: Friendly and informative; explains course options, schedules, fees, and discounts to a prospective student.
- [P3] **Insurance Call-Centre Agent**: Calm, reassuring, detail-oriented; handles a claim by asking for the customer's name and policy details, then explains what is and isn't covered.
- [P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary range, and grammatical accuracy; asks structured follow-up questions and gives no opinions of their own.

## 2. QUESTION POOL

### Band 5.0 - 6.0
- Q_5_01: Have you ever signed up for a membership at a library, gym, or club? What information did you need to give?
- Q_5_02: Do you prefer registering for things online or by phone? Why?
- Q_5_03: Have you ever taken a short course (like a summer course)? What was it like?
- Q_5_04: Do you have any kind of insurance, like health or car insurance?
- Q_5_05: What information do you usually need to give when you sign up for something new?
- Q_5_06: Have you ever called a company's customer service line? What was the problem?

### Band 6.5+
- Q_7_01: When calling customer service, what makes an interaction feel efficient versus frustrating? Give specific examples.
- Q_7_02: Do you think institutions (libraries, universities, insurance companies) collect more personal information than necessary nowadays? Why or why not?
- Q_7_03: How important are discounts and promotions (like "newly enrolled students get a discount") in influencing your decision to sign up for a service?
- Q_7_04: Insurance policies often have different "tiers" (e.g. Gold Policy) with different coverage. What are the advantages and disadvantages of tiered pricing systems?
- Q_7_05: Describe a time you had to explain a problem (like a car accident or billing issue) to a company representative. What made the conversation go smoothly or poorly?
- Q_7_06: To what extent should companies be required to clearly disclose what is NOT covered by a service or policy, rather than just what is included?

## 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL

### Band 5.0 - 6.0
- **to sign up for (something)**: to officially register or enrol for a service or course
- **membership**: the state of being an official member of an organisation (e.g. library, gym)
- **guardian**: an adult legally responsible for a child (used when registering minors)
- **to take down information**: to write down details someone gives you (e.g. name, address)
- **summer course**: a short educational course held during the summer break
- **currently studying**: in the process of studying right now
- **lecture / seminar**: a lecture is a formal teaching talk; a seminar is a smaller discussion-based class
- **discount**: a reduction in price, often for a specific group (e.g. new students)
- **insurance policy**: a contract with an insurance company describing what is covered
- **repair cost**: the amount of money needed to fix something (e.g. a car)

### Band 6.5+
- **to register for membership**: to formally join an organisation as a member
- **to include a guardian's name**: a requirement where a child's registration must list a responsible adult
- **newly enrolled students**: students who have just joined/signed up, often eligible for special offers
- **that lasts for (a period)**: used to describe the duration of a course or programme, e.g. "That lasts for a month."
- **to be involved in an accident**: to be a participant in an unexpected, often unfortunate event
- **to be covered by (a policy)**: to be financially protected under the terms of an insurance contract
- **in total**: used when summarising a final combined amount, e.g. "The bill is likely to be around £3,000 in total."
- **terms of (a policy/contract)**: the specific conditions and rules that apply to an agreement
- **keep in mind that...**: a phrase used to politely flag an important condition or limitation
- **to be unfortunate**: (of an event) regrettable or unlucky — often used sympathetically, e.g. "That's unfortunate."

## 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS

- Pattern_1 (Polite request for info): "Could you tell me [your name/library card number/policy details], please?" — a standard, polite way staff request personal information.
- Pattern_2 (Expressing sympathy before continuing): "Oh dear, that's unfortunate. I hope [positive wish]. [Follow-up question]." → e.g. "Oh dear, that's unfortunate. I hope nobody was injured. What happened exactly?"
- Pattern_3 (Explaining eligibility/condition): "[Group], / [benefit], / although [condition]." → e.g. "Newly enrolled students get a discount, although the offer only lasts this month."
- Pattern_4 (Describing frequency/duration): "There are [lectures/classes] [number] times a week, and [related activity] [frequency]." → e.g. "There are lectures three times a week and seminars twice a week."
- Pattern_5 (Clarifying what's included/excluded): "That includes [X], though not [Y] or [Z]." → e.g. "That includes breakfast, though not lunch or dinner."
- Pattern_6 (Reporting a problem, softening with 'the thing is'): "But the thing is, [problem happened]." → e.g. "But the thing is, I was recently involved in a car accident."
- Pattern_7 (Wrapping up a transaction): "OK, let me get a form ready for you..." / "Let's see... We have [option] this [time period]." — used to move the conversation toward action or a concrete offer.

# TOPIC: Home Town

## 1. PERSONA POOL
- [P1] **Proud Local**: Deeply attached to their home town, knows its history well, and enthusiastically recommends places to visit. Might get defensive if the town is criticized.
- [P2] **Recent Arrival**: Moved to the town recently for work or study. Still discovering the area, comparing it to their previous home, and might feel a bit homesick.
- [P3] **Ambitious Migrant**: Moved away from a small home town to a big city for better opportunities. Appreciates the city's dynamism but misses the peace and friendliness of their home town.
- [P4] **IELTS Examiner**: Professional, neutral examiner assessing fluency, vocabulary, and grammar.

## 2. QUESTION POOL
### Band 4.5 - 6.0
- Q_5_01: Where do you come from?
- Q_5_02: Can you describe your home town?
- Q_5_03: Do you know something about the history of your home town?
- Q_5_04: What is the weather like there?
- Q_5_05: Are there any interesting places I can visit?
- Q_5_06: What do you miss the most about your home town?
- Q_5_07: Can you compare your home town with big modern cities like Beijing?

## 3. VOCABULARY & COLLOCATION POOL
### Band 4.5 - 6.0
- **located**: situated in a particular place.
- **famous for**: known about by many people.
- **tourist**: a person who is travelling or visiting a place for pleasure.
- **capital**: the most important city or town of a country or region, usually its seat of government and administrative centre.
- **scenic spots**: places having beautiful natural scenery.
- **cloudy**: (of the sky or weather) covered with or characterized by clouds.
- **humid**: marked by a relatively high level of water vapour in the atmosphere.
- **stormy**: (of weather) characterized by strong winds and usually rain, thunder, lightning, or snow.
- **windy**: characterized by or exposed to strong winds.
- **helpful**: giving or ready to give help.
- **friendly**: kind and pleasant.
- **open-minded**: willing to consider new ideas; unprejudiced.
- **convenient**: fitting in well with a person's needs, activities, and plans.
- **transportation**: the action of transporting someone or something or the process of being transported.

## 4. GRAMMAR & RESPONSE PATTERNS
- Pattern_1: "My home town, [Name], is located in [Location]." (Stating location clearly)
- Pattern_2: "[Name] is a beautiful place with [Feature]." (Describing key features)
- Pattern_3: "The weather in [Name] is usually [Adjective]." (Describing weather)
- Pattern_4: "[Name] is famous for [Noun/Noun phrase]." (Stating what a place is known for)
- Pattern_5: "[Name A] is more modern than [Name B]." (Making a simple comparison)
- Pattern_6: "What I like the most, probably, is the food, I think." (Using a 'What' pseudo-cleft sentence to emphasize preference)
- Pattern_7: "In my opinion, [City] is developing too rapidly, so pollution has become a big problem." (Expressing an opinion and giving a reason/result)
